The harness holds no copy of the pattern and none of the layout, so a wrong belief on the kernel's side produces a mismatch rather than agreement. **It is the only check in this tree that observes something from outside the machine.**
  **One command longer than minimal, for a reason worth keeping.** Attaching backing gives the device an address it *keeps* across four later completed chains, so the interval in which a display owns a client's buffer is bounded by a pair of commands rather than by a virtqueue chain. A driver that reasoned about ownership the way the other two do — correctly — would hand the buffer back while a display was still entitled to read it.
  Two controls rather than one: the identical client with the submission removed must leave the screen blank, and a boot whose backing is displaced one page must fault a *read* and leave the same geometry with different contents — the sharpest of the three, because only the contents differ.
  *exit:* met. `cargo xtask gpu` prints `the kernel drew 16 x 16 with hash 0x6160417ef7842f25; the display showed 16 x 16 with hash 0x6160417ef7842f25`, and fails the command if either control produces the client's pixels.
  *needs:* E1-B01
- [x] **E1-B05** `L` Component supervisor: spawn from a manifest, restart policy, control ring delivery.
  **A manifest is compiled, not parsed** (RFC 0030), which is the decision this task existed to make before anything else. TOML has no business in a kernel with no allocator, and the tree already had the shape of the answer: `xtask` validates the schema at build time and emits a fixed-width `#[repr(C)]` record beside the component image, the content hash is over that record, and neither the frame nor the supervisor ever sees TOML. `cargo xtask lint-manifests` is still what refuses a manifest that stops fitting.
  Built and demonstrated at boot: a spawn that names a manifest by content hash and refuses when a declared need is not supplied; one control ring per component with the frame as its only producer; notices as pending state rather than a queue; and **endpoint as a place rather than an instance** — a connect to an empty place pends until a respawn refills it, which is the mechanism gate G1's sentence actually rests on.
  **The wall it hit is worth more than what it built, and it is the same wall `E0-B13` recorded.** A supervisor at ring 3 has to drive a control ring; driving one means adopting a mapped channel; `f_ring::Mapping::adopt` is `unsafe`; and a `user/` crate may not write `unsafe`. So the restart policy runs in the frame, where RFC 0008 says it does not belong. `component::policy::decide` was therefore written to take a record and a tally and no kernel state, so that moving it later is a move rather than a rewrite. The safe adoption a component needs is `E1-B08`'s, and the same wall is why `ANNOUNCE`, `PROGRESS` and the four capability calls are still on the door: RFC 0014's and RFC 0015's reversal conditions fell due here and were not paid.
  A spawn also grants into the child's table rather than deriving across tables, because the cross-table parent link RFC 0029 deliberately did not land. Until it does, what ends a component is the frame ending it, rather than the revocation walk reaching it.
  **A completion pass closed most of it** (RFC 0044, RFC 0047). A place is filled per component file with each account sized from that component's own manifest rather than from a constant, so the boot spawns two rather than one; `user/virtio-blk` is a scheduled ring-3 component; and the control ring demonstrably carries operations in both directions, with two opcodes implemented and answered from a frame polling loop and counted on every block boot.
  **What is still owed became a checked set rather than three sentences.** `cargo xtask lint-owed` reads `OWED_REVERSALS` and reports three reversal conditions that have fallen due and are unpaid — the restart policy still running in the frame, and RFC 0014's and RFC 0015's door retirements — each with the precise reason written where a reader looks. It goes red the day one is paid and the list is not updated, which is the difference between a debt and a wish.
  What changed about the door's debt is worth recording: the reason logged at first attempt was *a component cannot drive a ring*, and that reason is gone. The reasons that remain are different ones, and they are written in `abi/src/door.rs` rather than inherited.
  **The remaining half, characterised rather than estimated.** It was read out of the code on 2026-09-18 while trying to build it, and the shape it turned out to have is not the shape it looked like from outside. Recorded here so the next attempt starts where this one stopped.
  **A datapath boot contains two virtio-blk instances, and which one serves is now a parameter.** `component::demonstrate` builds a place for it on every boot — the log prints `place virtio-blk: private, on_fault, 8 restart(s) in 60000 tick(s), 4194304 B account`. `kernel/src/blk.rs` stands a second one up through `prepare_driver`, and on the six halves that are gates that second one is where the load goes. So this was never *build the missing thing*, it was *make one of the two existing things do both jobs*.
  **What is built, in the order it landed. All three of the acts `CHAOS_GAP` names.**
  A need may now be **supplied** by the caller rather than carved from the account — `Supplied` and `Placement` in `kernel/src/component.rs`, with `offer` sourcing a named need and checking its extent against the manifest. That is what a device window requires and why it could not be a flag on `carve`: a charged object is one `reap` hands back, and handing a bus address to the frame allocator would give the next component a disk controller.
  And **that place's occupant is handed a core, told where its device is, and reads it**. `user/virtio-blk/manifest.toml` declares a `board` need now, so the frame maps that component a routing page and writes the register span and the four structure offsets onto it; the occupant enters at `life::IDENTIFY`, reads the disk's capacity out of the device's own configuration structure, and writes it back. The boot prints `identified    place virtio-blk read its own device: 2048 sector(s) of capacity, outcome 7 — through the window its place was supplied with, from ring 3`, and `cargo xtask blk place` requires that number to be the disk it made. Until this, exactly one component in this tree had ever had a place's occupant run — the supervisor.
  **The first run of that check failed, and what it found is the reason the check exists.** `fill` handed `offer` the supply and handed `spawn` an empty slice. `offer` is what decides which needs the account does *not* pay for and `spawn` is what maps them, so the place held a capability for a device window that was in no address space — and every log line in the boot said the supply had worked, because every one of them was the frame's own account of what it had done. The occupant page-faulted at the third register page. One line; invisible for as long as nothing ran there.
  And **that occupant serves a client, concurrently, from inside `demonstrate`**. `cargo xtask blk served` is the third act. `component::serve_ring3` posts the job and `smp::start_on` starts the core *without waiting*; the frame is the client on its own core; `smp::join_serviced` answers the driver's translation requests for the whole of the join, which is the one thing a driver cannot do for itself because it does not own the remapping unit. The boot prints `served        place virtio-blk answered 3 entr(ies) and moved 1024 B for a client of the frame's, outcome 1 — from ring 3, on its own core, while the client submitted`, beside the frame's own `blk client    ... 2 translation(s) answered on the occupant's control ring, read back what it wrote: true`.
  **Four parties, four numbers, and no one of them can produce another's.** The frame says it started a core and answered two translations; the occupant says it took three entries off a ring and moved 1024 bytes, read back off the far half of its routing page under a magic the component writes last; the client says the bytes it read were the bytes it wrote. A build in which the translation route had quietly stopped being used would still move bytes and would publish zero for the one number only the frame holds — which is `claims/0012`'s discipline applied to a boot rather than to a claim.
  **What the third act cost, recorded because it was not what the estimate said.** Not `smp::start_on`, which already existed with four callers. Three other things: the occupant had no *data ring*, because `spawn` maps a control ring and the manifest declared no page for the ring this component serves on — so `user/virtio-blk/manifest.toml` declares a `data` need now, for the `board` need's reason one step on. `blk_place_supply` wrote twelve of the twenty-eight routing slots `laid_out` reads, and a page filled three-eighths of the way is a driver that refuses with `BAD_ROUTING` before it touches the device. And the client needed a way *inside* `demonstrate`, which is `component::Datapath` — injected rather than written in the frame's component module, because that file has no business knowing what a block request is.
  **The boot order was not the obstacle constraint 4 feared.** `blk_datapath` still runs before `component::demonstrate` and nothing in `main.rs` was reordered. What moved is the *client*, which now runs from inside `demonstrate` — so constraint 2 was satisfied by obeying it rather than by working around it.
  **The leak check was wrong in the harder direction, and the boot found it.** `Placed::stand_up` builds the device's domain *before* `demonstrate` takes its free-count baseline, so releasing it inside left the demonstration holding **more** frames than it started with — which the same equality catches and which is the harder direction to read. `Datapath::retained` now reports what the client took during the window, measured off the allocator rather than derived from the domain's own bookkeeping, and `Placed::tear_down` gives the domain back afterwards.
  **Four constraints the design has to satisfy, each found the hard way.**
  1. *One reservations table.* `demonstrate`'s own comment is the reason — *“A table built per admission would answer every demand as if it were the first, so a second hard-class component would be admitted onto cores the first already holds”*. So a `stand_up` that built its own tables for `blk` is ruled out; the driver's place must come from the same supervisor and the same `admit::table()`.
  2. *Places do not outlive `demonstrate`.* Its tail tears every one down through the same `tear_down` the scripted place reaches. So `blk` cannot be handed a place to use after `demonstrate` returns — the datapath stage has to run **inside** it.
  3. *The supply is conditional and per-device.* `virtio-blk`, `virtio-net` and `virtio-gpu` all declare `mmio` and `queues`, and an ordinary boot has none of those devices — `MACHINE` passes `-net none`. So a place is supplied only where this boot found the device, and every other boot keeps carving exactly as it does today.
  4. *The core is the same core.* `blk_datapath` takes `smp::first_worker()` and `demonstrate`'s `occupant_core` is the same one. They do not collide today only because they run in sequence.
  **What is not a constraint, which is the useful surprise.** The device window needs no reordering of `main.rs`: it comes from `remapping`, which is in scope well before `demonstrate` is called. The ordering problem is only about where the *client* runs.
  **The safe way in, and it is now the way in.** `cargo xtask blk place` and `cargo xtask blk served` are two halves that take the place-based path and are both deliberately **not** in `BLK_PROVOCATIONS`, so `cargo xtask blk` with no argument — the gate — still runs its three halves on `prepare_driver`. Each asserts exactly what it does: `place` that the place is supplied and its occupant read that supply back from ring 3, `served` that the occupant answered a client's entries concurrently and was asked for a translation. Neither asserts a driver that survives being killed under that load, and both success messages say so.
  **What is left of `CHAOS_GAP` is a sentence about the gate rather than about the mechanism**, and the row is narrowed rather than emptied for RFC 0036's reason. Six halves are gates — `blk=inside`, `outside`, `escape` and the three `deadline=` halves — and all six still stand an instance up through `prepare_driver`, so the occupant those halves can kill is not the occupant their load goes through. The needle stays until they move, which is `E1-P06`.
  *exit:* met, by the observation it was written as. The exit is `E1-P06` passes, `E1-P06` is a separate task that kills drivers *under sustained load*, and it is `[x]` one screen down — against an occupant of the place this task builds, which is the whole of why that exit was written as another task's observation in the first place. This is the third exit on this list written that way, after `E0-B12` and `E1-B01`.
  **What is still owed, and it is not this task's.** `CHAOS_GAP` keeps its row and its needle. Six halves are gates — `blk=inside`, `outside`, `escape` and the three `deadline=` halves — and all six still stand an instance up through `prepare_driver`, so the occupant *those* halves could kill is not the occupant their load goes through. That is a sentence about the gate rather than about the mechanism, the mechanism is demonstrated, and moving them is work with an owner rather than a wall: `cargo xtask blk place`, `blk served` and `blk killed` are the path, and the day the three provocations take it is the day the needle goes.
  *needs:* E1-D01, E1-D04, E1-B13 (a supervisor is the component the fixed table breaks on)
- [x] **E1-B06** `M` Deadline propagation across rings; every resource scheduler orders by the same field.
  **A device queue is ordered by what `inherit` returned** (RFC 0049). RFC 0025's rule was not re-decided; the driver keeps a queue on its own side of the virtqueue and hands the device whatever the shared function ranked first. The ordering has to happen there, because a virtqueue is consumed in the order the driver posts.
  **The cost is published as a number rather than hidden.** A request already handed to the device cannot be overtaken, so the granularity of an overtake is the depth kept in flight — that constant is written into the routing page, read back into the boot log, required to equal what the claim is bounded by, and named in the claim's threshold.
  **R08 is respected rather than routed around.** A class the submitting component was not admitted for is refused and never queued; a request served at the driver's own lower class carries a shortfall flag, and the boot fails if it does not. The manifest's declared class became load-bearing at run time rather than restated in the frame. The manifest's own prediction that this task would declare an admission-control feature bit is reversed with its reason: that bit means *will refuse a deadline it cannot meet*, this driver floors and reports, and declaring it would be R08's failure wearing a feature bit's name.
  *exit:* met. `cargo xtask deadline` is three boots of one client script. Ordered: six batch reads queued, a hard-class read arrives last and comes back first of seven, having overtaken six. Arrival, the control: the identical burst, read back sixth of seven, overtook none. Unadmitted: refused. The overtake is read from **both sides of the privilege boundary** — the frame's completion order and the component's own queue — and the verdict requires them to agree.
  A scar worth carrying: the pending queue was first sixteen entries of 64-byte-aligned records, a component's stack is one page, and it overflowed the guard page and killed the driver before it answered anything — presenting as *did not answer inside the bound*.
  *needs:* E1-B02, E1-D05
- [x] **E1-B07** `L` Admission control v1: CPU reservations with a schedulability test that can refuse.
  **One arithmetic, read by the frame, the simulator and the host tests**, so there is one schedulability test rather than three that agree until they do not. It tests all four of RFC 0007's components together and refuses in four ways, each naming the component that could not be satisfied.
  **The CPU half refuses against the frame's own clock rather than a utilisation bound**, and the reason is that RFC 0007's whole-core rule makes a utilisation sum have one term — a formula that refuses nothing, which is R08's *hint with a better name* wearing mathematics. What does refuse is RFC 0038's mechanism read as a cost: the frame reaches a given-away core only at its timer, so a period below one tick is a period the frame cannot observe.
  **The headline result is a refusal about the only machine this tree boots.** Where the part will not say what it is, the frame assumes the worst — an absent topology leaf becomes one contention domain covering every core — and so **QEMU cannot host a hard-class reservation, and this build says so** rather than granting it with a note. That is RFC 0007's *waive it and record it* alternative refused in the one place it would have been tempting.
  **A test that can only refuse is not a test**, so the boot asks the same arithmetic about a *described* machine with siblings and cache partitioning, which must grant, must record all four components as exercised, and must then refuse an over-subscription of it.
  *exit:* met, with two controls rather than one. Under adversarial load at one seed the granted arm meets 512 of 512 deadlines with no slot stolen; the same component with the same seed and the same adversary and **no reservation misses all 512** — which is what makes the first number mean anything.
  *needs:* E0-D04
- [x] **E1-B08** `L` User-level runtime: cores allocated to runtimes, preemption only at allocation boundaries, park-cleanly notice on reclaim.
  **A channel is adopted for a call, not for a lifetime** (RFC 0037), and that is what finally lets a component drive a ring in safe code. `Mapping::adopt` carries two obligations: that the region is mapped, which RFC 0033 already accepts and whose failure mode is a page fault; and that nothing else references the range, which a `Window` does not have to answer because a `Mapping` hands out real references borrowed from itself. The second is discharged structurally — every `Mapping` dies inside the call that made it — so `Adopted` keeps four words and rebuilds the mapping inside each accessor. The layout is believed once and never re-read, which is the hostile-peer answer that per-call re-adoption would have got wrong.
  **A core is allocated and a kernel entry is counted against it** (RFC 0038). An allocation is a set of cores and a reclaim promise per core, with no run queue; a reclaim naming a hard-class core is refused `ADMISSION/RESERVED` under RFC 0007. The reclaim fires on the runtime's own progress rather than on ticks, and the RFC says why: under TCG the same load measured between 18 and 260 timer ticks across runs, so a tick count is a property of the emulator.
  **The two exclusions are published beside the number rather than subtracted from it** — the `EXIT` that *is* the allocation boundary, and timer interrupts, which are the frame's clock reaching a core it gave away. R12: a concession is written as a cost, never hidden in a metric.
  `user/store` became a runtime: it adopts its control ring and its work ring in safe code, runs 16 384 work items through its own executor, drains notices at allocation boundaries and parks cleanly on reclaim.
  *exit:* met. `cargo xtask runtime` is four boots — load, where the hot path counts zero; provoke, where it counts one and equals what the component says it made; reclaim, where the timer posts under load and the runtime parks within a quantum with an empty queue; and hostile, where a scribbled header is refused `ARGUMENT/MALFORMED_HEADER` rather than believed.
  **What it did not clear, named rather than implied.** `kernel/src/blk.rs` still calls `driver.execute`, so RFC 0033's reversal grep still finds the frame; and `E1-B05`'s restart policy has not moved out of the frame. A component runs at ring 3 with its own polling loop now, which is the hard half — the two remaining moves are work, not a wall.
  *needs:* E1-B05
- [~] **E1-B09** `M` User-interrupt doorbell where the hardware offers it, with the kernel path as the negotiated fallback.
  **Owed rather than dropped, and owed to silicon.** Owed under RFC 0093, and the row is in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`. This task is *the hardware path*, and there is no half of it a virtual machine can stand in for: QEMU’s TCG backend implements no part of Intel UINTR and no `-cpu` model advertises the bit, so the instruction has never executed. `E0-B15` built the path to the point of **refusing to construct** — `Bell::new` returns `Err(Path::UserInterrupt)` rather than downgrading, because a channel negotiated into an instruction that faults is worse than a channel that refused — and that refusal is the whole of what can be exercised.
  The graph had `E1-B09` needing `E0-B15` and `E0-B15` deferring the hardware to `E1-B09`, which is a mutual wait that no commit resolves. Naming the silicon rather than the other task is what ends it.
  *exit:* **narrowed to nothing.** The clause — *both paths measured; the notification-cost claim is recorded with the hardware named* — is kept verbatim in the register. The second path has never run, so *both paths measured* is not a number this tree is short of; it is an instruction this tree has never issued.
  *needs:* E0-B15
- [x] **E1-B10** `M` Registered buffer sets, and the shared-virtual-memory path behind its feature bit.
  **Registration became an operation** (RFC 0028): two opcodes reserved at the top of every service's space, an entry whose every unread field must be zero, and a `SetId` answered in the completion. The service's table lives in the service's own memory and never in the shared region — the argument `E0-B15` made about doorbell counts, sharper here, because *a generation a peer can write is a peer that can un-revoke its own retired set*.
  **A slot retires rather than wrapping its generation.** Found in review: sixteen bits of generation and a wrapping counter would hand back, after 65 536 re-registrations, exactly the failure the generation was added to prevent — and no test in the suite ran that far, so the suite would have passed while the property it stands for did not. A slot that runs out is never filled again, and that has a test now.
  **Both paths are one test body**, not two that drift: `ownership_holds_on` takes the client's naming and the service's transport, and the suite asserts it ran over every declared path, so a path silently not exercised is impossible. The honest qualification is recorded in the code — the virtual path cannot refuse a double submission, and the test that would say so says why instead.
  *exit:* **met, narrowed under RFC 0093.** Both paths pass the same ownership tests, which is the half that says the mechanism is right. The cost is *registered* rather than measured: `claims/0004-buffer-registration-cost.toml` is `pending` with its thresholds written before any number existed, the workload runs, and `cargo xtask claim buffer-registration-cost` refuses to record — *container is not a measurement environment* — which is `bench/src/lib.rs` working rather than failing. **That clause is struck from this exit and kept in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`**, beside 0001 and 0002, waiting on the same machine. A threshold written before the number is the part of this that could be done from here, and it was done.
  *needs:* E1-D03
- [x] **E1-B11** `S` A splittable generator behind `Env`, before the simulator multiplies streams.
  `SeededEnv` runs on xorshift64 and `sim.rs` finalises its site draws with FNV-1a — both chosen for reproducibility, neither for statistical quality, and both say so. One seeded test cannot feel the difference; a nightly sweep of thousands of seeds across correlated streams can, and a sweep whose streams are secretly correlated explores less than it reports. A splittable design — SplitMix64-derived streams under a PCG- or SFC-class generator — is the standard answer, and the `Env` trait makes it a one-crate change. Seeds bind to a commit, so nothing recorded breaks: a new generator is a new commit. Before `E1-P01` and not after, because the seed corpus the simulator accumulates is priced in the generator it was drawn from.
  **One derivation, and streams that split by identity** (RFC 0026). `env/src/split.rs` holds SplitMix64's finaliser, a `derive(parent, identity)` that is injective in each argument, and an SFC64 `Stream`. `Stream::split` takes `&self` and derives a child from an identity while drawing nothing from the parent — so adding a stream in a later commit cannot move a stream that already exists, which is the property a recorded seed's survival depends on.
  The zero-seed guard is gone because the generator no longer has a fixed point to guard against, and that is tested by building the all-zero state by hand rather than asserted. The period floor is a guarantee from the counter rather than an average, and three named weaknesses are written down — which is what deleting *"chosen for reproducibility, not for statistical quality"* from two files actually costs.
  **One behaviour changed beyond the generator**, and it is the same bug in a second place: `wall()` used to draw from the live stream, so a stamped timestamp depended on how many values had been drawn first. It is derived at its own identity now.
  Every statistic is hand-rolled in integer arithmetic — there is no floating point left in `env/` — and each states what it does *not* detect. The correlation band is computed from the binomial rather than tuned until green, and a test requires the band to reject two copies of one stream, because a band that accepts everything is not a test.
  *exit:* met. The derivation is shared, the per-site independence test holds unchanged, and cross-stream correlation is bounded by a test that can fail.
- [x] **E1-B12** `L` The allocator the design names: buddy orders, per-CPU free lists, huge pages by default.
  `deadline-all-the-way-down` section 03 has specified this since before M1 and no task owned it — `mem.rs` says "this is the M1 floor" and points at a design document, which is a promise with no owner now that the floor is load-bearing. `Order` has been in every signature since M1, so the call sites are ready; what arrives here is orders above zero with split and coalesce, per-CPU lists so two cores allocating never meet, and `Order::HUGE` as the default grain. The drivers can run on order-0 frames through scatter-gather, so this does not gate them; what it buys is the huge-page default and uncontended allocation, both of which the datapath claims will price.
  **Coalescing is a pass, not a lookup** (RFC 0027), and that is what buys the buddy orders without a bitmap. `coalesce(budget)` takes an order's whole list, merge-sorts it by address in place — the blocks being sorted are the sort's only storage, bottom-up, no recursion, no allocation — and pairs adjacent buddies upward from order 0.
  **The bitmap was rejected by the per-CPU rule and not by its memory cost.** A bitmap is machine-wide and the lists are not, so two cores would either lock it or race it. Reading a buddy's own first word for a *free* tag was refused for a better reason: an allocated block belongs to somebody who may write anything into it, and an allocator whose correctness rests on a guess about somebody else's bytes has no correctness at all. The cookie stays a defence against corruption and is not promoted to an authenticator.
  **Clean and dirty hold at every order**, and the invariant is about bytes rather than labels: every byte of a block on a clean list is zero except the eight holding its masked link. A split writes no byte of either half except those link words, so an order-9 block's children are clean by construction; the coalescing pass zeroes the upper half's link before merging, because that word ends up in the middle of the merged block.
  *exit:* met. Nineteen orders with real split and coalesce under a seeded adversarial workload that returns the free count exactly to where it started; three counters published through the state tree say the hot path took no cross-core traffic, counted rather than asserted; and the M1 pair is gone rather than kept beside the new structure.
  *needs:* E0-B10
- [x] **E1-B13** `M` The capability table becomes an object: growth paid by `Untyped`, quota made real.
  `cap.rs` records its own reversal condition — a component that legitimately holds more than [`TABLE_SLOTS`], which is E1's first real supervisor — and the supervisor is this epoch. So the table stops being a fixed `PerCpu` array and becomes storage an `Untyped` capability pays for, which is the same change as giving a process a quota. The revocation walk stays iterative and bounded; what changes is whose memory bounds it.
  **A table is bought, a page at a time, by whoever holds it** (RFC 0029). `TABLE_SLOTS` stopped being the table and became the free part — the slots in the `PerCpu` static that let the frame fill a process's first handles before the process exists to spend anything. Above that, a table is bought pages recorded as addresses rather than pointers, so the type stays `Send` and `PerCpu<Table>` still compiles.
  **The order of operations is the bug avoided.** `derive` asks whether there is a vacancy *before* it retypes; growing after a successful retype would charge a component for a frame the call then refused to hand over. And `grant` deliberately does not grow: the frame has nothing to charge, and a grant that could buy a page on the frame's say-so is exactly the kernel reserve RFC 0008 refuses.
  **A bought page cannot be mapped, structurally rather than by a check.** Growth advances the same watermark a retype advances, so no later retype can hand out that frame and there is no `Frame` capability naming it to present to a map call.
  **Generations survive the bought boundary too.** A bought page is dropped when the process ends and the next process buys different memory for the same indices, so `clear_all` raises a per-table floor to the highest generation any bought slot reached. Without it, E0-B10's bug — a second process resolving a handle the first held — comes back at exactly the boundary nobody would think to test, so the forging fixture now holds a handle into a bought slot across a reset and requires `REVOKED`.
  *exit:* met. A process holds 544 slots where it held 32, the growth debited from its own `Untyped`; the five properties and the negative suite run at the new capacity rather than at the constant; and a process that cannot pay is refused `RESOURCE/QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`. The revocation walk is still iterative and still bounded, now by capacity rather than by a constant.
  *needs:* E1-D01
- [x] **E1-B14** `M` Shootdown batching, bought by a number or closed by one.
  Every revoke-unmap today is one page, one IPI, one spin on an acknowledgement — correct, and priced for a kernel that unmaps rarely. The datapath changes the rate: registered buffers cycle, and a driver restart unmaps a component's whole grant page by page. Batching is what mature kernels do here, and it is also exactly what rule 3 forbids designing before the measurement exists — so the workload comes first and the number decides.
  **The number decided, and it decided against the axis the task named.** The workload came first, as ordering rule 3 demands: a client cycling registered buffer sets and a driver restart retiring a whole grant, through the shipped registry and the machine's real remapping unit, with a host twin beside the other datapath workloads and a check that the two report the same counts.
  **The churn issues zero shootdowns and zero IPIs, and that is a property rather than an omission.** A registered buffer set is a *device* translation, so retiring one edits the remapping unit's tables and no processor's; an instance's address space is never in `CR3`; the only path that shoots down is a capability revoke, which is not a datapath event. So one-page-one-IPI was already under any bound worth stating on this workload — because on this workload it performs none. The zero is a finding and not an absence, because the boot prints the machine's running total beside it and requires *that* to be non-zero.
  **The axis beside it was loud, and `vtd`'s own module comment had already named this task as its arbiter.** Every page of every retired set made the unit discard everything it had cached, globally, twice. Batching per request rather than per page: 320 invalidations to 40, 640 register round trips to 80, 87.5% gone. Sound rather than merely faster, because the invalidation is global and the contract an unmap owes is about the moment it returns — and the one thing made worse, the window from the first cleared entry to the invalidation, is written as a cost rather than left out (R12).
  Both policies stay in the tree and both run in one boot, so undoing the batching goes red rather than going quiet.
  *exit:* met, in the second of the two forms the exit allows — and on a different axis from the one it predicted, which is the outcome ordering rule 3 exists to make possible. The p99 half is `pending` on `E0-D10`'s machine, and its `[hardware]` block states the extra reason: an emulator answers an invalidation instantly in software, so a time taken here would measure QEMU's dispatch loop wearing a hardware unit's name.
  *needs:* E1-B02
- [x] **E1-B15** `M` The state tree in every component, not only the frame.
  `docs/the-long-plan.html` has had layer L8 at "E0 v0, E1 everywhere" since the layer was added, and `E0-B14` delivered the v0 — the frame's nodes in a read-only mapping. E1 put more *into* that tree without changing who owns it: the three drivers and the runtime each hand the frame a tally and the frame publishes it under its own root. Two places in the tree already record that as the debt it is. `user/store/src/report.rs` explains why a runtime reports an exit status instead of a tree and writes its reversal as *a runtime that publishes a tree of its own*; RFC 0038 reads a component's ring cursor from the frame and says the honest version is *a state tree the component publishes under RFC 0013*. No task owned either reversal, which is the promised-layer-with-no-owner decay this file polices. It matters here because RFC 0013 names its own test of worth — *whether E1's fault sweeps and E2's state comparison actually consume it* — and `E2-P05` cannot compare two whole-system states while only the frame publishes one. Found on 2026-09-06 while checking E2's readiness rather than E1's.
  **The declaration moved into the manifest, which is what makes a refusal possible before the component runs.** RFC 0065, schema 2 → 3: `[[state]]` carries a node's permanent id, name, kind, unit and parent, `abi::manifest::Record` grows a 28-byte `Node` array, and a spawn charges a third fixed part to the account, lays the header and schema out of the record with `f_abi::state::publish`, and maps it writable at `process::SPAWN_TREE`. The alternative — a constant inside the component's own image — cannot be refused before the component runs, and a component that has already run has already escaped the refusal. The frame writes the description and writes no word after it, so a component spawned and never scheduled has a readable tree full of zeros: the difference between *has done nothing* and *cannot be read*.
  **One root, and the mount is read back through the node that was just written.** The frame's tree grows a `components` subtree, four mount slots and three counters (32 → 41 nodes); `kind::MOUNT` is a node whose word is the physical address of another published region; and `demonstrate` requires mounted == spawns and every mount non-empty at the end, so a gap fails the boot rather than being reported. A component that publishes nothing is refused first in `admit` — `ADMISSION/NO_STATE_TREE` — provoked on every boot against a record whose only difference is an emptied declaration, so a build that lost the refusal publishes zero in `components/refused` and goes red. Boot: 4 places, 5 spawns, 5 trees mounted carrying 25 nodes, 1 refused for declaring none.
  **The sweep consumes it, which is RFC 0013's own test of worth.** `f_abi::state::Region` reads a tree out of bytes with the same `check`, `validate` and `fold` the `Reader` uses over a mapping, and an abi test holds the two against one buffer; `sim/src/state.rs` gives a modelled component a region its counters *live in* — `App`'s `issued`, `completed` and `ended` are gone as fields — and `fault.rs`'s `peergone` scenario asserts the class's whole system response out of nodes and out of no record at all, rather than out of the serial log.
  **The builder's own caveat, kept rather than rounded up: every published word is currently zero.** Nothing yet schedules the four components through the path that would write them. The tree is readable, its shape is the component's, and its numbers are not yet anybody's — which is exactly the state the design intends for a component that has not run, and is not yet evidence about a component that has.
  **The schema-3 collision, recorded here because this is the line that kept the number.** `E2-B05` and `E1-B15` were built in parallel worktrees, neither able to see the other, and both needed the manifest: both bumped `schema` 2 → 3 and both wrote an RFC 0065. Nothing warned them, because the only thing that would have is a number allocated somewhere both could read, and a worktree is by construction not that place. Neither declaration supersedes the other, so schema 3 carries `[[device]]` *and* `[[state]]` with both bounds and both doc paragraphs, and `Record::BYTES` is 2696 — neither worktree's number, because 2248 and 2680 were each computed without the other's fields. **The merge found that both writers had stamped their count at byte 101**: a silent corruption neither branch could have seen alone, and the reason this is a paragraph rather than a footnote. `devices` keeps 101, `state_nodes` takes 102, two of the record's three reserved bytes are spent and one is left, and `every_structural_lie_is_refused` now breaks byte 0 because that is the only reserved index there is. The doubled RFC number was fixed rather than preserved — 0065 stays with the state tree, which had twenty-seven citations against the driver entry's sixteen, and the driver entry became 0067; `docs/rfc/README.md` carries the reasoning, the rows and the lesson, which is that two parallel worktrees touching one numbered registry will both take the same number and the only defences are to allocate the number before the branch or to expect the merge to resolve it. **No line was added to `CLAUDE.md`'s *Common mistakes*, and the judgement is written down rather than left implicit:** that section's rule is *added when the same mistake happens twice*, and two agents making one mistake simultaneously — neither able to learn from the other — is one occurrence with two symptoms rather than two occurrences. A line saying *do not take a registry number in a worktree* would also not have prevented it: both agents did check the tree they could see. If it happens again, that is the second time and the line is earned.
  *exit:* partly met, three clauses of four, and the fourth was deliberately not faked. *Every component the supervisor starts publishes a tree of its own under RFC 0013's rules, mounted under one root*: met, with the caveat above — the shape is published and mounted for all five spawns, and every word in it is zero. *At least one scenario in the `E1-P02` sweep asserts its system response by reading a component's subtree rather than the serial log*: met, `fault.rs`'s `peergone`. *A component that publishes nothing is refused at spawn rather than tolerated*: met, in `admit`, and provoked on every boot rather than only in a test. **What is not met is the reversal clause.** `user/store/src/report.rs` still packs a tally through the door and RFC 0038 still reads a component's ring cursor out of the frame, because `kernel::runtime` builds a runtime's process a different way and does not map this page. Half of that reversal is paid — `user/store/manifest.toml` now names the four nodes `Tally`'s fields would become, and the frame writes that schema into the component's own address space at every spawn — so the debt is one mapping away rather than one design away, and both sentences that state it stand unchanged where they were written. Whoever closes this line pays that mapping; nothing else on it is owed.

  **"One mapping away" was measured on 2026-09-09, and it is one mapping and a text page.** The mapping half is small and it works. `kernel::process` gains an `OWN_TREE` page after `WORK`; `prepare_runtime` allocates and maps it as a fifth part, which `PARTS_MAX` was written as a maximum precisely to make a one-constant change; and `kernel::runtime` publishes the manifest's schema into it through `component::publish_tree` — the same function the spawn path uses, answering the packed refusal rather than that module's error so it can have two callers — then reads the words back before `reap` takes the page. **Mapped rather than granted, and that is not a preference:** the four grants a runtime is given are load-bearing, because `door::Entry::granted` computes the nth handle from the first at the first's generation, and the comment on the fourth grant already records what a fifth cost the last time two shapes disagreed. A page in the map loop costs no grant, which is also how `SPAWN_TREE` arrives.
  **What it runs into is the component's side, and the number is the finding.** A component may not write `unsafe`, so the store path has to live in `abi` as a `state::Writer` beside `Reader` — and every byte of it is compiled *into* the component, because a component links as one library and `f_abi`'s functions are `#[inline]` for exactly that reason. On this tree at this commit:
  ```
  store image as it stands                        3904 bytes
  what the runtime shape maps for text            4096 bytes
  slack                                            192 bytes
  with a Writer that validates the schema         4632 bytes
  with a header-only bind and one schema pass     4440 bytes
  ```
  So the write path costs about 536 bytes against 192 of headroom. Trimming it to fit means hard-coding the data offset in the component, which is the second copy of the arithmetic `abi/src/state.rs` exists to prevent, and leaves the next edit to `user/store` to break the boot with no slack left. The close this task actually needs is the one `xtask`'s own refusal names — *widening it means widening `kernel::process`'s own reservation in the same diff* — and that moves `GUARD` and everything derived from it, so the addresses `user/init` and `user/store` hold as constants move with it, and `prepare_runtime` needs a second text frame to copy an image across. That is a layout change with its own blast radius rather than a line in this one.
  The attempt is not in the tree and was reverted deliberately: a `Writer` shaped by a 192-byte budget is the wrong API to leave behind for a frame that is about to stop having one. What is in the tree is this paragraph, so that the next person starts from the measurement rather than from the sentence above it, which is now withdrawn.
  **Paid on 2026-09-11, the way the measurement said it would have to be.** `INIT_TEXT_PAGES` is four pages and `xtask`'s `INIT_MAX` is the same number written twice; `process::OWN_TREE` is the fifth part `prepare_runtime` allocates and maps; `kernel::runtime` publishes the manifest's schema into it through `component::publish_tree` before the first instruction and reads the words back before `reap` takes the page; and `f_abi::state::Writer` is the write side, in `abi` because a component may not write `unsafe` and a mapped page can only be reached through a raw pointer. RFC 0072 carries the decision and the ceiling — the chain is the text reservation plus nine pages and has to stay below `SPAWN_GUARD`, so seven is the most this could ever be and four leaves three spare.
  **What the frame can check now that it could not before, and it is the half that would have bitten.** `user/store`'s `CONTROL_AT` and `WORK_AT` were private constants inside a module gated on `feature = "image"`, which the kernel does not enable — so a disagreement between the component's layout and the frame's was a page fault at the first adoption with nothing in it naming the cause. They are in `f_store::report` now, the one module of that crate the frame links, and `kernel/src/runtime.rs` carries three compile-time assertions against `RING`, `WORK` and `OWN_TREE`. That is what the three driver crates have had since RFC 0047 and this shape did not, and this change is exactly the kind that needed it.
  *exit:* met. `cargo xtask runtime` boots four halves: load, provoke and reclaim each move the tree's snapshot with two of five declared nodes carrying a non-zero word, and the hostile half leaves it **unmoved** with none — a scribbled control-ring header is refused before the component does any work, so *unmoved* is a real answer rather than a missing one. The two readings are the evidence and neither is evidence alone: the first is taken before the component's first instruction, so *moved* says the component stored something rather than that the frame happened to hold something already. A count of non-zero nodes is reported and not a named value, because the frame reads a manifest for shape and not for meaning.
  *needs:* E0-B14, E1-B05
- [x] **E1-B16** `M` The shared half of three driver supervisors becomes one module, and `Reported` stays three.
  RFC 0051 named this as what a third driver was to have merged, and RFC 0054 refused it for a reason scoped to `E1-B04`: the merge rewrites the file a closed task's evidence rests on, inside a task whose own evidence is a picture on a screen, so a defect it introduced would be found by the wrong check or by none. As a unit of its own every clause of that is false — `blk`, `net` and `gpu` each carry a control that must fail, all three are CI jobs, and `deadline` carries a gating claim — so the merge's own evidence is exactly the evidence it disturbs, which is the condition RFC 0054 was asking for.
  **Measured before it was decided**, which is the half RFC 0071 exists to record. With comments stripped: `Registers` and its two methods, `struct Supervising`, `declared` and `order_for` are byte-identical in all three files; `impl Supervising` is byte-identical in `net` and `gpu` and differs in `blk` by one method that is the general one specialised at a fixed timeout; and `struct Reported` is **not one type** — `blk`'s carries `capacity`, `overtaken`, `queued_max` and `in_flight`, which the other two have no counterpart for, because a block request has a depth and an order and a display command does not. Four move, one moves with a four-line repair, and one stays.
  **Two things it must not do, both of which would be green.** It must not merge `Reported` behind a shared prefix: that moves four of `blk`'s fields by eight bytes, every writer uses the symbolic name so a full rebuild is safe, a partial one is not, and the magic check would still pass on a stale image reading shifted fields. And it must not move `prepare_driver(` out of `kernel/src/blk.rs`, which is the needle `CHAOS_GAP` keys on — deleting it turns that check red without closing the gap it declares, which is worse than no check.
  *exit:* met. `kernel/src/supervisor.rs` is 460 lines and the three driver files are 960 lighter between them. All four suites pass on both trees, twelve boots each, and the comparison is **212 of 213 result lines identical**. The one that moves is `net teardown`'s *turns of the receive poll found nothing*, which is a busy-poll spin count and therefore a time; the semantic half of that same line — receive buffers given back as cancellations — matches exactly, 0, 1 and 0 on both sides. Everything else that differs is the kernel image being smaller: five more free frames, and the allocator and state-tree counters that follow from them. `lint-owed`'s RFC 0051 row names `Reported` alone.
  **What the merge nearly did silently, and what caught it.** `cargo fix` removed the imports the moved code took with it, and where a `const` was used as a *match pattern* and had gone out of scope it renamed the pattern to `_NEED_MMIO`. That is not an unused-variable fix: a binding in pattern position matches **any** value, so the arm reading the register-page need would have swallowed every need in the manifest and the arm below it would have been dead. The unreachable-pattern warning on that next arm is what reported it. The two constants are the same two bytes in all three drivers and are in the shared module now.
  *needs:* E1-B04

### Prove — this is the epoch where the testing environment becomes real

- [x] **E1-P01** `XL` **The deterministic simulator.** Virtual time, seeded scheduling and ordering, device models for blk, net and gpu, and component substitution.
  **The simulator runs the components, not the instructions** (RFC 0032), and that is a reinterpretation of this exit rather than a way of meeting it. The kernel cannot be built for the host — its own `Cargo.toml` argues why — so `sim/` models the system *above* the frame and QEMU keeps the frame. The RFC says the cost out loud: the simulator will never catch a bug inside the frame's own algorithms, and the word *boot* in this exit is answered by a different command than the word *workload*.
  **A modelled device is a peer on the real ring types** (RFC 0034), which is what stops the models being a parallel universe that agrees with itself. Seventeen scenarios now run under `cargo xtask sim`, each reproducing byte-identically at its seed and moving when the seed moves — compared on the trace text and the decision log, not only on a digest.
  **Review found the third false pass of this epoch, and it was in the check built to prevent exactly it.** RFC 0035 defined the run as a pair over *one component set* and built `sim --join` to require it. The check was one-directional: it asserted every component the boot spawned was among those the simulator ran, and nothing the other way — and its other half compared the component directory with itself, which is the failure RFC 0035's own text says it rejected. Meanwhile the boot builds one place from the first module, so the halves were about `{store}` and `{store, virtio-blk}` while the artefact header claimed coverage of both.
  RFC 0036 is the answer and it is the shape this list should prefer: the join is symmetric now, and the difference is a *declared set* — `JOIN_GAP = ["virtio-blk"]` — required to match exactly. A component appearing there that nobody declared goes red, and so does one still declared after the boot starts spawning it. A silence became a quantity.
  *exit:* met, under the reading RFC 0032 argued and RFC 0035 made checkable. The workload half reproduces from `(seed, commit)`; the boot half is `cargo xtask trace --hash`; and the pair is now over **one component set** — `join: ok — 2 of the 2 component(s) the simulator ran were spawned by this boot, and the 0 that were not are the gap this tree declares: none.` `JOIN_GAP` is empty and the check is symmetric, so it goes red if the two sets ever diverge again in either direction.
  What this does not claim is written into the artefact rather than left to a reader: the frame's instructions run in QEMU and nowhere else, so the simulator will never catch a bug inside the frame's own algorithms. RFC 0032 says so in its Consequences and names what covers that instead.
  *needs:* E0-P02, E1-B11 (the seed corpus is priced in the generator it was drawn from)
- [x] **E1-P02** `L` Fault classes in the simulator: allocation failure, translation fault, device page-fault latency, peer death mid-operation, torn doorbell, partial write, delayed completion.
  **A fault class states its response** (RFC 0039), which is the whole difference between this task and a fault injector. A scenario that injects and prints what happened is an observation somebody has to read and judge, and next month nobody does. So the response is written first — this request is refused with this code in this domain, this peer's death is seen as latency and not as loss, this torn doorbell costs a wakeup and not a completion — and then made true.
  Seven classes, seven scenarios, seven tests each named for the response it asserts: `alloc`, `mapfault`, `faultin`, `peergone`, `doorbell`, `partial`, `latecqe`. Four of them are only observable through the block device's status byte or its address decode, which is why they all run against it.
  **Both load-bearing properties are tested rather than assumed.** A class's answer is a pure function of its seed, its label and *its own* occurrence — there is a test that interleaves other classes and requires the trajectory not to move — and arming a class that never fires leaves every scenario's records and decision log exactly as they were. That is `env/src/sim.rs`'s argument about a seed expiring silently, held at the new scale rather than restated.
  *exit:* met. Each class has a scenario in `cargo xtask sim`, each asserts its response, and each reproduces at its seed and moves at eight different ones.
  *needs:* E1-P01
- [x] **E1-P03** `M` Nightly seed sweeps: N seeds across M scenarios, with automatic minimisation of any failure to a reproduction command.
  **A sweep needs an oracle that does not name the bug** (RFC 0040). The five properties in `sim/src/check.rs` are expressed only over what reaches a client and name no scenario, no fault class and no defect — because a check that knows what it is looking for finds only that. A finding's signature is the *first* property to fail, which is what keeps the signature stable while the run shrinks underneath it.
  **The minimiser preserves the signature rather than the failure**, and the distinction is the one that makes it correct: neither `after` nor `one_in` is a subset operation, so a candidate that still fails may be failing for a different reason. A move is accepted only when the same check fires. Running it twice on one failure gives the same answer, and that is a test rather than a hope.
  **Time and parallelism are where a sweep is allowed to want them, and they cannot reach a result.** `f-sim` reads no clock — `lint-determinism` scans it with no allow-list entry, so it could not — and its output is a function of its arguments alone. Two tests hold it: one sweep at one worker and at five must produce one identical report, and the mutation harness runs the armed sweep in two separate processes and requires byte-identical output.
  **The defect is in the shipped source behind a feature that is off by default**, extending RFC 0017's argument from the frame to the layer above it. From the third entry of a harvest the modelled device answers with the first entry's token — reachable only after two consecutive seeded coalescing decisions with work still behind them.
  *exit:* met. `cargo xtask sweep --mutate` arms the defect, runs 288 trials, reports 13 findings, and prints `repro  git switch --detach <commit> && cargo run -q -p f-sim -- --trace --seed 0xd8901edeb3f44762 blkloss` — then disarms and requires the sweep to go quiet. It is in `cargo xtask verify`. 1 152 trials in 0.9 s warm; the nightly default is 65 536 seeds, 1 179 648 trials, 268 s.
  **The seed corpus exists and the release row went green**, which retires the conditional-content mechanism RFC 0021 introduced: the `Requirement` enum is deleted and `cargo xtask release --dry-run` reports 8 of 8. That is what RFC 0021's *what would reverse this* asked for, and it named this task as the owner.
  Declared rather than left to be found: every corpus entry today was found under the deliberate defect and each line says so; the defect does not *need* a sweep to be caught, and the sweep earns its keep on two scenarios and on producing the smallest reproduction; and minimisation preserving a signature is not a proof that what it removed was irrelevant.
  *needs:* E1-P02
- [x] **E1-P04** `L` Hostile-peer fuzzer: a peer that writes arbitrary values to the shared header and cursors, restarts mid-operation, and lies about its epoch.
  **A hostile peer is generated, and a hang is a count** (RFC 0046). The peer is drawn from a seed through `split`: arbitrary bytes and named fields in the header and its reserved words, cursors that run backwards, jump, exceed the ring or sit near the `u32` wrap, forged index-ring slots, a scribbled consumer flag word, and a peer that restarts with a bumped epoch — including between a batch being staged and published.
  **A run is episodes, and that is what makes a billion reproducible.** Each episode's stream is derived from `(seed, index)` by identity per RFC 0026, so a finding at operation 999 999 999 replays in a millisecond from `--seed <base> --episode <k>` rather than by re-running the first billion. The generator decision made at `E1-B11` paid for itself a third time.
  **A hang is a count, not a wall-clock timeout**, because a timeout in a test is a flake generator. And the reach guard is not a tautology: twenty-six paths have minimum counts, and a short run fails naming the three it did not reach.
  *exit:* met, with one substitution argued in the RFC rather than hidden. `cargo xtask hostile --exit` performs 1 000 000 512 operations in 49 s — 158 060 094 refusals caught, 44 M peer restarts, 12 M observed epoch changes — with no panic, no hang, and no unsafety. The substitution: this is a host test over `f_ring`, which is the same code the frame calls on its peer-facing path, and the frame *calling* it is one boot rather than a billion. Memory unsafety is checked under Miri at a much smaller count, and both numbers are reported rather than one being quoted.
  `mutate-believed-header` proves the properties can fail: armed, the panic property finds it with a one-episode reproduction, and no other property does.
  *needs:* E0-B13
- [x] **E1-P05** `L` Structure-aware submission-entry fuzzer with coverage feedback; the corpus is committed to the tree.
  **Fifteen named families of entry answered in eight named worlds** — the frame's executor, and a registration service whose table is empty, live, lent, revoked, full or generation-exhausted, and the shared-virtual-memory transport. A case is self-contained, so a corpus line is a whole input and replays at the cost of one case rather than of the run.
  **The structure-aware fraction is measured rather than asserted**: 87.5% structure-aware to 12.5% byte-level, floored in the claim, and the reason is arithmetic — an `Sqe`'s first check is that the reserved word is zero, so sixty-four random bytes fail it with probability 1 − 2⁻³², which is exactly how a byte-flipping fuzzer spends a billion draws learning nothing. `E1-P04` had already demonstrated that.
  *exit:* met. The validation path is defined as a written list of functions rather than left to mean whatever a percentage needs it to mean, coverage of it exceeds 95%, and the corpus is committed and released beside `E1-P04`'s rather than in a third format.
  *needs:* E0-P03
- [x] **E1-P06** `M` Driver chaos: kill each driver component at random under sustained load.
  **The first gating claim in this project.** `claims/0005-driver-restart-blast-radius.toml` is `gating` — a regression fails the build — and it can be, because it is a count rather than a time: `clients_observing_failure` at most 0, with operations lost, answered twice, answered wrongly and places retired all at most 0, and operations in flight at the moment of the kill at least 1, so a run that killed nothing cannot pass. The latency half is split out as `claims/0006`, `pending`, with its threshold derived from the manifest's declared 10 ms first backoff before any number existed — rather than folded in and thereby weakened.
  **"No client observes anything except added latency" is decomposed into three separately-failing claims plus a bound**, because a harness asserting the sentence whole asserts nothing: nothing lost, nothing answered twice, nothing answered wrongly, and a latency that is bounded and reported. A tail with no bound is a hang with better manners.
  The mechanism gate G1 rests on is exercised once per kill: the client re-registers through the endpoint it already holds, and that registration *pends* against the empty place until the restart policy — read out of the component's own compiled manifest record — refills it.
  **The other half is paid, and the thing being killed is now the thing serving.** `cargo xtask blk killed` takes the occupant of the *place* — spawned from its manifest into an account with every need checked, holding a device window the account could not carve, serving the frame from ring 3 on a core of its own — away from its client while it has work outstanding, and requires the client to have lost nothing. `killed        place virtio-blk was taken away from its client with 1 operation(s) outstanding: Killed { vector: 14, error: 20, ... }`, then `refilled      place virtio-blk epoch 1`, then `blk survived  ... 0 buffer(s) never got their bytes`.
  **The kill is asynchronous by construction, and that is what it cost to build.** Everything else that ends a component in this tree is something the component did — a fault it took, an exit it chose, a stop it agreed to — and all three are cooperative in the one way that matters: the component reached a point of its own choosing. A driver that crashes under load does not. So `component::fault_occupant` withdraws the occupant's text and shoots the translation down, and error `0x14` — user, instruction fetch, not present — is what says the kill landed where it was aimed rather than the component having gone on its own.
  **`f_ring::PeerGone` grew the second source its own comment had been promising since E1.** A buffer the device holds leaves only through its completion or through `reclaim`, which demands evidence the completion is never coming; the type's doc said that evidence "arrives with `E1-B05`". It has: `PeerGone::told`, and `Killer::kill` does not answer with one until the occupant's core has reported finished.
  **Two bugs, and neither was visible until something was killed.** `process::TEXT_PAGES` is the address space set aside for text and not what `spawn` maps, so refusing on the first page that was never there made a kill that killed nothing and reported it as a core that had wedged. And **supplied memory broke the promise a restart makes**: the restart line says *nothing carried over — new table, new memory, new control ring, new state tree*, and everything the account pays for gets that free because `fill` allocates zeroed. Supplied memory does not, because the caller holds it across the boot precisely so the *same* run reaches the next occupant — and the same run is the previous occupant's leavings. The second driver laid a virtqueue over the dead one's rings, the device refused its first read with `DEVICE`, and every log line above it said the refill had worked.
  *exit:* met, and the two halves are taken in the two places this project takes them. *Kill each driver component*: the simulator, where `cargo xtask chaos` kills every component the build produced and `claims/0005-driver-restart-blast-radius` is `gating` on four decomposed rows plus a bound. *At random under sustained load*: the simulator again, which is where this project's fault and load numbers have always been taken. What the boot adds is the clause that entry could not make for itself — the occupant being killed is the occupant a client's load is going through, on a real device, across a real remapping unit.
  What is **not** claimed: this boot kills once rather than at random, and one driver rather than each. `KILLS_MAX` is four and the manifest's budget is eight in sixty thousand ticks, so the room is there; what is not there is a reason, because the count is the simulator's job and the boot's job is the *kind* of thing being killed.
  Watching a declared gap shrink three times, in the words of the check rather than in a commit message, is the argument for declaring them at all.
  *needs:* E1-B05
- [x] **E1-P07** `L` Kani on the capability properties — the same five the negative suite asserts, proved rather than sampled.
  **The five properties are proved over the file the kernel ships**, not over a copy of it: `kernel/proofs` compiles `cap.rs` a second time through a path attribute against three stand-ins small enough for a checker to finish (RFC 0053). Handles are unbounded — every harness takes all 2³² — and rights cover the whole 256×256 lattice including undefined bits. **Table contents are bounded by construction rather than by assumption**: a harness never writes a slot, it runs the real grant, derive, grow and revoke with symbolic operands, so no proof holds for a state the table cannot reach.
  Assertions are two-sided, because a one-sided property is satisfied by a table that refuses everything.
  *exit:* **met, and the first word is now observed rather than asserted.** `cargo xtask prove` runs the harnesses and **seven deliberate defects each fail the harness stating the property they break** — the clause that matters, since a proof passing on a build with a known defect proves nothing. It was six; a third proof crate arrived with `abi/proofs` and brought one more.
  **This entry said the schedule could not be observed from here, and that was wrong.** It read *“whether it runs in CI is a statement about a scheduled job, and this machine cannot observe one”*. `gh` is installed and authenticated, and `gh run list --workflow nightly.yml` lists the scheduled runs on `main`. Run **35323010047**, 2026-09-18, job *the capability properties and the ring’s validation paths, proved* — conclusion **success**, ending `prove: ok — 31 proof(s) hold, 15 of them at both bounds, and 7 deliberate defect(s) each fail the harness that states the property they break, where they break it.`
  **That run is red overall, and citing a green job inside it is deliberate rather than careless.** It failed on a different job — *a generation broken, rolled back, and compared three ways* — for a reason with nothing to do with the proofs: `MAX_MODULES` was one short of a boot menu offering two generations. A job’s conclusion is the evidence for the clause about that job; the run’s conclusion is evidence about the run. Reading the second for the first is how a green tick comes to mean less than it says.
  Kani needed its own toolchain and its own rustc, which is RFC 0022's arrangement taken one step further; the image build needs host networking, and that is written down rather than rediscovered.
  *needs:* E0-P08
- [x] **E1-P08** `M` Simulator snapshot and restore, so a long scenario bisects in seconds rather than hours.
  **Split-by-identity paid for itself a second time, for a reason nobody had asked about when it was bought.** RFC 0026 made streams derive from an identity rather than from a parent's output, so what a snapshot has to carry for the ordering stream and the seven fault streams is an occurrence count per site — a dozen pairs, no generator state, no tree. It held for two of the three; the exception is the one that is genuinely a chain, and that is recorded rather than glossed.
  **Every module that holds state gained its `save`/`load` beside its fields**, so the author of a new field sees the two lines they owe. The default `save` refuses by name, which means a crate that has taught some actors and not others refuses the runs containing the others instead of writing a short file that would restore into a plausible, wrong world.
  A snapshot is data on disk and therefore a wire format: versioned, and a snapshot from another build is refused rather than misread (R04).
  *exit:* met, and the saving is shown in wall clock rather than asserted. `claims/0007-snapshot-re-entry-saving.toml` registers the ratio `pending` with a threshold of ten, and says in its own words why a ratio measured in one command on one machine is less fragile than a bare timing without pretending that makes it publishable here.
  `Simulation::run_to` stops *between* two steps, which is R05 collected as a dividend: a system whose events all arrive at one polling point has a well-defined between.
  *needs:* E1-P01
- [x] **E1-P09** `M` Write the test taxonomy: for each class of bug, which layer catches it and how often that layer runs.
  **Eighty-five rows**, each naming the bug class, the layer that catches it, the command that catches it today, how often that command runs, and a status. Nineteen are gaps, and every one of them ends in a task id that schedules it or a sentence that accepts it with a reversal condition.
  It has a machine-readable twin, `docs/test-taxonomy.toml`, with the same rows — so the table can be checked against the tree later rather than rotting quietly, which is what a taxonomy written once in prose does.
  The row the seven layers have no home for is written down rather than omitted: *provoke the real kernel and require it to fail*, which is where most of this project's evidence currently comes from.
  *exit:* met. `docs/test-taxonomy.md` and `docs/test-taxonomy.toml` agree row for row.
- [x] **E1-P10** `M` Claims for the datapath: ring submit under load, doorbells per operation, copies per operation, kernel entries per operation.
  **Two of these four are counts this machine may take, and the tree spent a while getting that number wrong in both directions.** `DATAPATH_GAP` and `RELEASING.md` said *all four are times*, which turned buildable claims into a purchase order. The correction that replaced it said *three* — and that was wrong too.
  *Copies per operation* and *kernel entries per operation* are bytes and entries a deterministic kernel produced against an emulated device, identical on any host that runs the binary. *Doorbells per operation* has a count’s unit and a time’s behaviour: `ring/src/doorbell.rs` says the number the design cares about is **zero under load**, and whether a producer needs to ring depends on where the consumer is in its drain — under TCG, the emulator’s scheduling of two vCPUs. `E0-B15` had already declined to register the boot’s figure for that reason, and *500 per 1000 operations* out of a two-operation self-test is the artefact it was declining. So two of the four wait on `E0-D10`, not one.
  So this line is **work rather than debt**, and most of the evidence already existed: `kernel/src/blk.rs` asserts `copies == 0` on every datapath boot with a provocation behind it, so the counter is not a zero a deleted mechanism would also publish.
  **One of the three is registered.** `claims/0036 copies-per-operation` is `gating`, over nine rows and all three datapaths: a copies count that must be zero, a denominator that must not be, and a provocation that must not be. The three boots now print those rows beside the prose line they already printed, **on `Half::Inside` only** — every other half moves a different number of bytes, and a denominator printed by all of them reaches `measured_rows` as one name with two values, which it refuses rather than averages. The positive control is the half that moved the client’s bytes and got them back, so it is the half with the authority for what a working datapath copied; a refusal has no copies-per-operation to report.
  Measured: `blk 0 of 1024`, `net 0 of 106`, `gpu 0 of 1024`, each with 512 bytes moved through the same function on purpose. `cargo xtask claim copies-per-operation` is in the pull-request gate, which `lint-claim-runs` is what requires.
  **Both takeable counts are registered.** `claims/0037 kernel-entries-per-operation` is the second: 16 384 work items through a component’s own executor, zero crossings on the hot path, one at the allocation boundary that ends the residency. Its provocation is unusually good and was already in the tree — `Half::Provoke` is not a second configuration but *the same load* with one door call made deliberately, so the pair is a zero and the same counter moving under the same work rather than a zero and an assertion. The two provoke rows are counted on opposite sides of the boundary and the boot requires them equal.
  A wrong number was caught on the way: the claim’s first draft read `report::PROVOKE` as a count of door calls and wrote *two*. It is a selector ordinal, and the provocation makes one. Running the boot before writing the thresholds against it is what caught it — the order `E0-D04` and RFC 0084 argue for, here costing a sentence instead of a claim.
  The other two wait on `E0-D10` and are in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`: *ring submit under load* because it is a time, and *doorbells per operation* because it is a count whose value is set by scheduling — `E0-B15` had already declined to register the boot’s figure for that reason, and this file spent two corrections agreeing with it.
  *exit:* **four claims, gating, each with a tuned-Linux baseline where one exists** — narrowed under RFC 0093 to **two gating counts and two owed to a machine**, which is the split `claims/0012` and `0013`, and `0014` and `0015`, already use where a task produced both. Both counts gate: `claims/0036 copies-per-operation` over three datapaths and `claims/0037 kernel-entries-per-operation` over a component’s own executor, each run by the pull-request gate, which `lint-claim-runs` requires of anything marked `gating`. *A tuned-Linux baseline where one exists* is met by there being none for either — both `[baseline]` sections say `system = "none"` and argue it, rather than naming a configuration nobody has. The two owed are kept in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`.
  *needs:* E1-B09
- [x] **E1-P11** `M` Cross-architecture CI: the AArch64 job builds and runs the same suite under emulation.
  **The architecture list is derived from the workspace** (RFC 0045), which removes the failure this task existed to remove: `cargo xtask test` cross-compiled a hand-written list of crates, and a hand-written list stops matching the workspace silently — `xtask` already carried a comment recording that exact thing happening once, to `f-bench` and `f-init`. Every crate is now either built for AArch64 or excluded with its reason in the source, and a new crate is included by default.
  **Then the review found the gap the crate list leaves**, and it is the difference between the two halves of this exit: a crate can be on both runners with an architecture `cfg` on a test *inside* it, so the job stays green while one machine collects fewer tests — and a test count is not an assertion. `cargo xtask lint-arch-tests` reads the gate where it is written, including on a `mod` declaration and everything that file declares in turn, because `user/init/src/component.rs` carries no trace of the gate that excludes it.
  *exit:* **met, and what changed is where somebody looked rather than anything in the tree.** No test is skipped on AArch64 without a recorded reason, and that is now checked rather than reviewed. *Green* is a statement about a job that runs only on an arm runner; no such runner and no way to execute an AArch64 hosted binary exists on this machine or in the container, so nothing local can observe it. It is the same honest gap `CLAUDE.md` already records — the AArch64 job is where the ring's ordering means anything, and nothing local substitutes for it. **Every sentence above is still true, and not one of them was a verdict on this task** — *not observable from here* is not *not observed*, and the observation was one `gh run view` away for as long as the sentence stood unread. **Run 34370629685**, the merge of #31 into `main` on 2026-09-09, carries `tests (AArch64, weak memory)` and `memory-ordering litmus (AArch64, weak memory)`, both green, on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`. **The counts came out equal too**, which this task's own paragraph says a green job does not establish: 48 test binaries and 862 tests on x86-64, 48 and 862 on AArch64, in that run. `cargo xtask lint-arch-tests` is still what asserts it and the equality is only corroboration — but the failure that paragraph predicts, green on both runners while one collects fewer tests, is the one thing an equal count rules out, and it is ruled out. **The title keeps *under emulation* rather than being edited to match the runner.** RFC 0045 already resolved that phrase to the arm runner rather than to a local command, and `ci.yml` states the reason at the job: TCG serialises rather than reordering, so an emulated run would be green whatever the ordering said. Native silicon is the stronger check and not the equal one, so the word stays and this paragraph is what a reader comparing the two should find. `ARCH_RUN_GAP` is untouched and stays true: it is a statement about *this machine*, which has not changed, and closing this line on a runner is precisely what it was written to permit.
- [x] **E1-P12** `M` Kani on the ring's validation paths: panic-freedom proved, not sampled.
  `E1-P07` proves the capability properties; the same tooling reaches the other structure a hostile peer feeds bytes to. `pop`, `take`, `Layout::adopt` and `execute` each promise that nothing a peer writes produces a panic, and today that promise rests on fuzzing that samples and a clippy wall that guards this crate's own code. A bounded proof over arbitrary header bytes, cursors and entries is cheap for code this small, and it is the difference between "no fuzzer found one" and "there is none".
  **The fixture is a region of bytes, not a struct of fields**, and `ring/src/mapping.rs` had already named that trap: a channel assembled from fields a harness owns can only ever be laid out correctly. So the proof hands 640 symbolic bytes to the real `adopt`, and the header, both cursor pairs, the flags word, the index ring, both entry arrays and the arena are then *the same bytes*. Cursors and index-ring slot numbers are unbounded. That is what makes the unchecked index a proof obligation rather than a fuzzing target.
  **A cover that cannot be satisfied is a failed proof** — a lesson from the first draft, where a harness verified in under a second because nothing it drew was ever adopted. `E1-P04` had already spent a billion hostile operations on these same paths and found nothing, which is the sharpest possible statement of what this task adds: a billion samples is still sampling.
  It links the crate rather than recompiling a file out of it, because `f-ring` is an ordinary `no_std` library that already builds for the host — so there is no second copy to drift.
  *exit:* **met, on the same reading as `E1-P07`, and every number in the sentence that stood here was stale.** It said *27 harnesses across the two crates … 13 of them at both bounds, with six deliberate defects*. `PROOF_CRATES` holds three — `kernel/proofs`, `ring/proofs` and `abi/proofs` — and the observed nightly reports **31 proofs, 15 of them at both bounds, and 7 deliberate defects**. Reintroducing the unchecked index fails a proof and not only a boot.
  The four paths this task is about are each proved rather than sampled: `popping_an_arbitrary_entry`, `taking_an_arbitrary_completion`, `adopting_arbitrary_bytes` and `executing_an_arbitrary_entry`, verified in run 35323010047 with every cover property satisfied.
