The entry’s own sentence that the promise *“rests on fuzzing that samples and a clippy wall”* was true when written and is not true now.
  **What stays open is declared rather than owed**, and it is `RING_PROOF_BLIND`: four mutations a sequential checker cannot see — `mutate-relaxed-submission`, `mutate-relaxed-completion`, `mutate-no-doorbell-fence`, `mutate-reusable-slot`. Kani explores one interleaving, so a weakened ordering is invisible to it by construction. That is `E0-P16`’s subject and not this one’s, and `lint-proofs` prints the four on every green run so the gap is read rather than assumed closed.
  *needs:* E1-P07
- [ ] **E1-P13** `M` The scaling claim: N clients on N cores, one ring each, and the number of cache lines two cores share per operation.
  The share-nothing design is enforced by a lint and exercised by nothing. Every mutable static is a `PerCpu<T>`, the allocator's free lists are per core, the ring is single-producer single-consumer per client, and RFC 0016 holds the set of addresses two cores reach at four words plus RFC 0063's routing word — and `init` runs on core 1, each driver runs on core 1, and `MAX_CPUS` was raised to 64, measured, and put back to eight because *a ceiling is not a speedup*. So the property the whole architecture is priced on, that throughput on N cores is N times throughput on one because no two cores write the same line, is a design intention with a lint behind it and no number in front of it. This task is the number, and it comes in two halves for the reason `E1-P06` split its claim: a count is gating on the machine this tree boots, and a time is not.
  **The counted half runs under QEMU and gates.** N runtimes on N cores, N from two to whatever the machine offers below `MAX_CPUS`, each driving its own ring at its own core through the allocation `E1-B08` built, and the counters `E1-B08` and `E1-B12` already publish read per operation: `RUNTIME_HOT` at zero, `MEMORY_REMOTE` less `MEMORY_FORCED` at zero, shootdowns and non-timer interrupts at zero, and the four-plus-one cross-core words the only addresses two cores reach — asserted by the boot, not by a reader counting `PerCpu` declarations. A machine that offers one core is a refused measurement rather than a passing one, because a zero taken with nothing to contend is `MEMORY_FORCED`'s argument in reverse.
  **The time half is registered `pending` with its thresholds written before the number**, on the `E2-P10` pattern, and it waits for the machine `E0-D10` names: operations per second per core at N cores within five percent of the figure at one core, and cache-line transfers between cores per operation from the part's own snoop counters as the secondary metric, because TCG has no cache to miss in and a number taken there measures the emulator's dispatch loop. The baseline is a tuned Linux with one `io_uring` per core and `SO_REUSEPORT`-style sharding, configured by someone trying to win, in `claims/baselines/linux-6.x-tuned` beside the ring-submit baseline rather than a second directory that says the same thing.
  What this task does not claim, said before it is tempting: nothing about a cross-socket machine, which `runner-class-A` excludes on purpose; nothing about a core class, which is `E6-B01`'s; and nothing about what a shared L3 slice costs, which is the placement question this claim's per-core number makes askable and does not answer.
  *exit:* the counted half is a `gating` claim with every counter above at its bound across N of two and more, and the boot refuses on a machine with one core; the time half is registered `pending` on `runner-class-A` with the thresholds and the baseline directory named, and a raised `MAX_CPUS` has a number that says what it bought.
  *needs:* E1-B08, E1-B12

### Release

The scenario set, what a seed means, what a finding looks like and what to do with one are carried in the programs' own help output rather than in a document, because a document drifts from a program and help text does not.

  The entry already argued this was the right shape for a release task to have, comparing it to `G4` — the only gate in the long plan whose outcome is deliberately not under the team’s control. That argument is why this closes rather than waits: a task whose remaining criterion is somebody else’s decision is a task that will never close on its own terms, and holding the graph open on one teaches a reader to stop reading the marker.

The datapath chain this exit used to name — `E1-P10` ← `E1-B09` ← `E0-B15` ← hardware — is retired: two of those claims gate today and the other two are rows in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md` under RFC 0093, which is what a release names rather than waits for. Nothing was tagged, pushed or published: the release gate blocks those unless a variable names an authorisation, and an agent cannot set that for itself — a gate the gated party can open is a log entry.

> ### Gate G1
> A driver is killed under sustained load and the system does not notice.
> A bug injected into any component is found by an overnight seed sweep and
> arrives as a reproduction command rather than as a symptom.

## E2 — State

*Phase 02. The object store, the declarative system, and the two properties
that follow from them: rollback that works and update that does not reboot.*

**Effort:** 1.5–3 person-years · **Risk:** medium · **Ends at:** gate G2, release 0.3

*Where the tree stands against this epoch, checked 2026-09-06.* Gate G0 is
unpassed with eight E0 tasks `[>]`, and E1 is built but not proved: all six
decisions and eleven of fifteen builds are `[x]`, the simulator and its sweeps
are `[x]`, and the release is `[>]` with its blocking chain named on the line.
What E2 leans on and has: virtio-blk in user space (`E1-B02`), the simulator
with device models and snapshot (`E1-P01`, `E1-P08`), the frame's state tree
(`E0-B14`), eighteen registered claims with the first gating one a count rather
than a time (`claims/0005`), the measurement history (`E0-P11`), and a SHA-256
and deterministic archive writer, which `E0-R01` put in `xtask/src/pack.rs` and
`E2-B01` has since split — the hash into `hash/`, where a `no_std` store can
reach it, and the archive writer left where it was. What it
leans on and does not have: a supervisor whose restart policy has left the frame
(`E1-B05`, still `[>]` with the reversal owed under `lint-owed`), registered
buffers measured (`E1-B10`), a tree each component owns (`E1-B15`), and
zoned-device emulation — the development image's QEMU is bookworm's 7.2, and
`docker/README.md` names the one-line base change to trixie that `E2-B02` and
`E2-P10` will need. Two things that decide how E2's numbers get taken: every
timing claim in the registry is `pending` until `E0-D10`'s machine exists,
while `claims/0005` shows a count can gate on the machines this project has —
so the write-amplification and re-chunking numbers (`E2-P10`, `E2-B09`) are
countable here and the read-path latencies are not. Several tasks below
carried no `needs:` and so ranked as ready to start; the graph now says what
they wait for.

### Decide

- [x] **E2-D01** `M` Write RFC 0012 — an update is a generation swap, and the root hash is the attestation.
  **The number this line reserved was the point of reserving it.** `TODO.md` held 0012 open when the E2 line was written, and the directory ran 0011, 0013 for two epochs while forty-six entries were written around the hole. A reader arriving at a gap cannot tell a reserved number from a withdrawn one, and the difference is the whole of what this task was: a withdrawn entry is a decision somebody unmade, and this was a decision nobody had made yet. `docs/rfc/README.md`'s reserved-numbers section is where that is now visible without opening the file.
  **A root is a hash over artefacts and over nothing else, and the RFC says so before a reader can put more into the word.** It carries no signature, names no verifier, proves nothing about a frame already modified to lie about itself, and says nothing about mutable data. What it does buy is the metric, and the metric is two counts rather than a sentence: `reboots` is required to equal the frame-changed predicate exactly, so the claim fails in an artefact rather than in an argument.
  **Two later commits in this slice could not have been written without it**, which is ordering rule 1 working rather than being cited. `hash/` moved SHA-256 out of `xtask/src/pack.rs` because this entry decided that a release address and a blob address are one identity; `abi/src/store.rs`'s `component` leaf has a fixed width because this entry answered the `ContentId` question first. Written after either, both would have been guesses that were expensive to correct.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0012-an-update-is-a-generation-swap-and-the-root-is-the-attestation.md` is accepted, and the rollback metric is now *one generation swap, and a reboot only when the frame changed* — a reboot in exactly one case, the incoming root record's `frame` field differing from the running one's. `intent/0006-state/plan.md` step 1.
- [x] **E2-D02** `M` Mutable extents: the design for the workload content addressing is worst at, written as a second object kind rather than as a unification.
  **RFC 0058 falsified the spec's own arithmetic before anything was built**, which is the most a decision at this position can do. An earlier draft of `intent/0006-state/spec.md` put the extent kind's re-chunking threshold at exactly `EXTENT_BYTES` = 1 MiB. A write of `W` bytes at offset `O` rewrites `ceil((O mod EXTENT_BYTES + W) / EXTENT_BYTES)` pieces, which is two whenever the write straddles a piece boundary — about 0.39% of 4 KiB writes at uniformly drawn offsets — so a one-piece threshold would have gone red on a wholly legal workload as soon as the bench ran long enough, and the obvious repair would have been an aligned-offset workload, which is fitting the measurement to the threshold. Claim 0017's extent rows are 2 MiB with `pieces_touched_max` = 2 beside them, and the straddling write is its own recorded row.
  **It refuses a unification out loud and prices the refusal.** The same bytes stored as an object and as an extent have two different hashes; a consumer picks a kind when it creates the object and changing its mind costs a full rewrite. Two shapes carried honestly, rather than one shape that covers both badly.
  **What it does not do is resolve the complication**, and the class it is bad at turned out to be the size RFC 0058 said it was. RFC 0061 wrote that half of it had gone — that periodic content below the target size is served by the chunked kind under the new acceptance rule — and RFC 0062 withdraws that sentence on 0061's own confirming run: a period below `CHUNK_MIN_BYTES` = 16 KiB is starved under every boundary rule this design permits, and the database page and the freshly-provisioned image repeat at 4096 and 8192 bytes. So both halves stand, and the periodic half is now the *stronger* of the two — a zero run is starved because one register fixed point misses one drawn mask, which a different draw would change, while a short period is starved by an argument no draw and no rule reaches. This entry's decision did not need that, and what it has now is a reason that does not depend on the chunker's parameters at all. The reversal condition is unchanged and points the other way: if `E2-B09` measures the class too small to justify two object kinds, this entry is wrong.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0058-a-mutable-extent-is-a-second-object-kind-and-not-a-unification.md` is accepted and names all three: the granularity is `EXTENT_BYTES` = 1 MiB with one whole piece as the copy-on-write unit; the snapshot boundary is an explicit snapshot taken by the component that owns the extent, with no hash naming the current bytes between snapshots and a reader by hash seeing the last snapshot whole; and the workload it is bad at is small random writes to large mutable objects, priced at up to 256 bytes re-copied and re-hashed per application byte and up to 512 across a piece boundary. `intent/0006-state/plan.md` step 7.
- [x] **E2-D03** `S` Garbage collection policy: mark from live roots, sweep by live fraction, batch class, roots pinned explicitly.
  Sits immediately before `E2-B02` by ordering rule 3: a collector built without its policy is the work that is expensive to redo, and the three invariants are cheaper to state before the code exists than to reverse-engineer from it. `E2-B02` is not in this slice and the entry landed anyway, for that reason and one more below.
  **The invariant that would have been wrong is the first one.** The root set is not the pinned roots; it is the pinned roots *and every open write set*. A publish registers a transient root at its first `ZONE_APPEND` and releases it only when the completion of RFC 0060's second `FLUSH` has made it durable *and* that root has entered the pinned set, in that order. Without the transient root the collector is correct only while nothing is publishing, and the argument for it would have had to be *the sweep is careful about in-flight publishes*. Care is not assertable; a root set is.
  **The third invariant got a number, because without one it is not an invariant.** A hard-class read submitted while a sweep is in progress reaches the device with at most `collector_operations_ahead_of_hard_read` = 1 collector operation ahead of it, and the 1 is `f_virtio_blk::pending::IN_FLIGHT` — claim 0012's chain length, not a number chosen here to be satisfiable.
  **`SWEEP_LIVE_FRACTION` = 0.25 is owed before the claim that spends it**, which is the second reason this landed early. Claim 0016's `device_bytes_per_app_byte` ≤ 1.5 is derived from it — 1.0 for the data plus `f/(1−f)` = 0.33 copy-forward bytes per new byte, plus padding and root-record overhead, totalling 1.39 with the rest said as margin — so the constant had to exist before `E2-B02` registers the number that spends it.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0059-a-collector-is-three-invariants-and-a-batch-class-consumer.md` is accepted, and all three are written as predicates over named state — the root set `R`, the mark bitmap `B`, each zone's state and live byte count, and the driver's queue — so `E2-P03` asserts a predicate at every step of a run rather than watching for a symptom. `intent/0006-state/plan.md` step 7.
- [x] **E2-D04** `M` The state-transfer protocol a component implements to be updated in place, and what it declares when it cannot.
  **The declaration is merged and the swap is two builds away.** RFC 0063 is accepted and `abi/src/transfer.rs` is the wire: `Declaration` is sixteen bytes inside `abi::manifest::Record` — `mode`, `schema`, `record_bytes`, `records_max`, each with its unit — in two modes and not three, because a third value meaning *transferable only from a named quiescent point* is what `in_place` already means. `[transfer]` is **required** the way `[restart]` is, and a zeroed `mode` is refused by `Record::read` rather than read as `restart_only`. That is one word further than this task's plan paragraph went — it called `restart_only` *the honest default* — and the entry refuses the default: a reader of a manifest cannot then tell *decided `restart_only`* from *never thought about it*, and those two differ exactly at the moment somebody refills a place from a newer manifest.
  **The schema went 1 to 2, and every component file in the tree was rebuilt.** That is the reversal inside this task and the reason a schema got an RFC rather than a field: RFC 0030 priced a bump as a rebuild of every component file, and `docs/manifest.md` said a schema other than 1 is refused. Four manifests are the whole cost — `user/virtio-blk` at `in_place`, `user/store`, `user/virtio-gpu` and `user/virtio-net` at `restart_only` — because nothing has ever shipped a component file, which is why the bump landed here rather than at `E2-B06`. `Record::read` refuses a schema that is not 2 and a `mode` that is zero; `cargo xtask lint-manifests` refuses a file with no `[transfer]` table and checks `record_bytes * records_max <= memory_bytes` on the one that declares them, because the transfer window is bought out of the incoming component's own `Untyped` and never out of the frame. The bookkeeping half of the bump is `E2-D06`, one line down.
  **`user/virtio-blk` declares `in_place` and did not have to be rewritten to do it**, which is the question the exit's second clause turned on. `component::serve` already reaches a point where `Pending::is_empty()` is the whole of what the driver has accepted and not answered, because `pending::IN_FLIGHT` is one and `Driver::execute` polls its chain back out of the device before it returns. The argument is written in the manifest beside the declaration rather than in a commit message, and it is a property of that constant rather than of the design: `E1-B09` raising `IN_FLIGHT` is RFC 0063's first reversal condition, and it has an owner.
  **The third clause is paid, and it was paid in the simulator.** `E2-P08` swapped it on 2026-09-07: `cargo xtask swap` replaces `user/virtio-blk` in place twice under sustained load, the thirty-two-byte record `user/virtio-blk/src/state.rs` crosses a real transfer window, and the `SetId` the client already holds keeps working — `claims/0029`'s `registration_sets_replayed` = 2 and `operations_redone_in_place` = 0 beside `operations_redone_by_restart` = 24. **RFC 0063's own reversal condition was read against that run and is not met**: its central sentence is `E2-P08` observing a dropped, doubled or wrongly-answered operation across a swap, and the run observed none of the three with three negative controls in shipped source proving the counters can move. What is still unpaid is not this decision but the frame's implementation of it — `SWAP_GAP`, on `E2-B06`'s line — so this closes as a decision whose protocol has two implementors and one caller. The exit line below is the verdict as it stood before that run and is kept rather than rewritten, because a task's exit is evidence about the day it was written.
  *exit:* partly met, on two clauses of three, and the third is not startable from here. The exit is *"schema merged; the virtio-blk driver from E1 declares one, and E2-P08 swaps it"*. *Schema merged*: met — `abi::manifest::SCHEMA` is 2, the declaration is in the record, and `the_record_layout_matches_the_abi` reads `abi/src/transfer.rs`, so an offset cannot move on one side alone. *The virtio-blk driver from E1 declares one*: met — `mode = "in_place"` in `user/virtio-blk/manifest.toml`, with the quiescence argument beside it. *And E2-P08 swaps it*: not met and blocked — `E2-P08` waits on `E2-B06`, which waits on `E2-B05`, which waits on `E1-B05`, and no command in this slice reaches it. Until something swaps, this is a schema and not yet a protocol, which is RFC 0063's own reading of itself: its central reversal condition is `E2-P08` observing a dropped, doubled or wrongly-answered operation across a swap, and nothing has swapped anything. `intent/0006-state/plan.md`'s *The rest of E2* paragraph for this task, which named `abi/src/transfer.rs` and none of the schema bump's other five files.
  *needs:* E2-D01, E1-B02 (the exit names the driver that declares the first one)
- [x] **E2-D05** `S` Write RFC 0060 — a publish is a barrier sequence, and atomicity is not free; the five blk opcodes that make it expressible.
  **This line did not exist until after the decision had landed, and the id is new rather than a renumbering because ids here are permanent.** `intent/0006-state/spec.md` found the gap, its plan declined to close it — a plan may not edit this file — and the RFC's own *Affects* says "No task line in `TODO.md` names this RFC; that is reported to the originator rather than fixed by editing that file". This is the originator acting on that report. Until it was written, the only record in the tree that a reversal had been paid was a row in `docs/rfc/README.md`, because `cargo xtask lint-owed` reports reversal conditions that have fallen due and not RFCs that are owed a line.
  **The wire went first, which is ordering rule 1 rather than a preference.** `FLUSH` = 3, `ZONE_APPEND` = 4, `ZONE_FINISH` = 5, `ZONE_RESET` = 6 and `ZONE_REPORT` = 7 are five numbers two peers will read, and a number is free to change while one peer exists and expensive once two do. They landed in `user/virtio-blk/src/driver.rs` carrying no behaviour: the driver still answers two opcodes, refuses these five with `ARGUMENT/UNKNOWN_OPCODE`, and a test asserts both that refusal and that `op::known` does not admit them — so the day `E2-B02` widens `known`, the widening is a red test rather than a silence. `cargo xtask blk` staying green is the evidence that nothing else came with them.
  **What it reverses is a published sentence.** `docs/design/deadline-all-the-way-down.html` section 04 says *atomicity is free because a root is a single write*. A publish is four device operations — write the blobs, `FLUSH`, append the root record, `FLUSH` — and the two barriers are what make the record's meaning true and then make the record itself durable. Because a device may acknowledge a barrier it did not honour and nothing above it can tell, correctness does not rest on the barrier at all: a root record is believed only after its `check` field verifies and every child of its generation node resolves. The barrier makes a lost publish rare; verify-before-accept makes a lost publish a rollback to the previous generation rather than a generation naming blobs that are not there.
  **What a reviewer should check before `E2-B02` builds on it:** the four zoned virtio request types the RFC maps these onto — `ZONE_APPEND` 15, `ZONE_REPORT` 16, `ZONE_FINISH` 22, `ZONE_RESET` 24, with `FLUSH` on `VIRTIO_BLK_T_FLUSH` = 4 — are written from knowledge, because there is no copy of the virtio specification in this tree or in the development image. A wrong mapping is exactly the class of error ordering rule 1 exists to make cheap now and expensive later.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0060-a-publish-is-a-barrier-sequence-and-atomicity-is-not-free.md` is accepted and the five opcodes are fixed constants in the blk service's opcode space. The design page's sentence is not yet edited; that row is owed by three RFCs at once and is carried under `E2-B01`, which is the open task whose diff brought all three. `intent/0006-state/plan.md` step 2.
  *needs:* E1-B02 (the opcodes land in the driver E1 built, and its boot is what says they cost nothing)
- [x] **E2-D06** `S` Write RFC 0063 — a transfer is declared in the manifest, quiescence is asserted by its occupant, and the manifest schema goes 1 to 2.
  **This line exists for the schema bump, and the id is new rather than a renumbering because ids here are permanent.** It is `E2-D05`'s shape one entry later and for a narrower reason. `E2-D04` owns the protocol and cannot close until `E2-P08` swaps a driver, which is two builds away; the bump is a reversal that landed with the declaration and closed with it, and nothing in this file named it. RFC 0030 priced a schema change as a rebuild of every component file and `docs/manifest.md` said a schema other than 1 is refused — both already written down, which is what makes a schema field a reversal by rule. A decision whose only record is an open task line is one a reader cannot tell from an unanswered question, and `cargo xtask lint-owed` reports reversal conditions rather than owed RFCs, so nothing else in the tree would have said it.
  **What the bump costs, and why it lands at a decide task rather than at the build that needs it.** Sixteen bytes per component file and every offset after byte 104 moved, both compile-time constants mirrored in `xtask/src/manifest.rs`, with `the_record_layout_matches_the_abi` going red when either side moves alone. Four manifests and a rebuild inside this tree; zero outside it, because nothing has ever shipped a component file. That second number will never be zero again, which is the whole argument for paying now rather than at `E2-B06`, and it is the same argument ordering rule 1 makes about a wire number: free while one peer exists, expensive once two do.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0063-a-transfer-is-declared-in-the-manifest-and-quiescence-is-asserted-by-its-occupant.md` is accepted; `abi::manifest::SCHEMA` is 2; `[transfer]` is required by `Record::read` and by `cargo xtask lint-manifests`; all four component files in the tree are rebuilt at schema 2 and `cargo xtask verify` is green on them. `docs/rfc/README.md` carries the row, against `E2-D04` because that is where a reader looks for the decision.
  *needs:* E2-D01, E1-B02 (the same two E2-D04 has, and for the same reasons)

### Build

- [x] **E2-B01** `L` Blob store: content addressing, content-defined chunking, the on-disk format.
  The hash was already chosen once, for the release address, host-side. The store needs the same function under `no_std`, and it should be the same function — a release address and a blob address computed by two algorithms is two notions of identity where RFC 0012 wants one.
  **One identity became one implementation.** `hash/` (package `f-hash`) is that function: `no_std`, no allocator, checked against FIPS 180-4's published vectors, and `xtask/src/pack.rs` now calls it rather than carrying its own transcription. `pack::hex` stayed in xtask and f-hash has no hex function, because `hex` returns a `String` and f-hash has no allocator — so f-hash's tests compare `[u8; 64]` arrays, and any later `no_std` caller that wants a printable digest brings its own formatter.
  **One published vector could not move verbatim, and moved stronger.** `pack.rs` hashed a million-byte vector in one call; with no allocator the same published digest is asserted over a million bytes fed in hundred-byte pieces, a length dividing neither the 64-byte block nor the total. Writing the empty-and-short-`update` case found a real bug on the way: a short `update` onto a non-empty buffer would have discarded the buffered bytes and hashed a shorter message than it had counted.
  **Both halves are built now.** `blob/` (package `f-blob`) carries the gear table and a chunker that is three words, holds no bytes and hashes as it scans, with `Cuts` finishing its slice on `Drop` so that a partially consumed iterator cannot make an object's boundaries a function of the shape of the loop that read it. The format half is `intent/0006-state/plan.md` step 6 and landed after it: `abi/src/store.rs`'s superblock, blob header, object head and root record, each refusing on its magic, then its declared length, then its discriminating field before returning any field at all; `blob/src/device.rs`'s `Device` trait with `Memory` behind it and the barrier on the trait from the first commit; `blob/src/store.rs`'s `put`, `get`, `put_object`, `get_object` and the first half of RFC 0060's publish sequence. This tree has now formatted a device, which is the sentence RFC 0061 said made its reversal cheap while it was false.
  **What the exit run measured, at `--blobs 1000000` from seed 1.** A million blobs of 217 354 639 content bytes into a modelled device of 1 003 529 blocks of 512 bytes — all 1 003 529 written, two barriers — and 1 000 000 records for 1 000 000 blobs, so nothing was deduplicated by accident: the run draws at least 32 uniform bytes per blob precisely so that a store which folded everything into one record and read it back a million times fails this instead of passing it. Then 1 000 000 verified, 0 refused, 0 wrong bytes without a refusal. Then the control, which is the half that is worth anything: one bit flipped inside one stored blob's *content*, all million read again, exactly **1** refused — with `refusal::CONTENT`, at the blob that was corrupted — and **0** blobs returning bytes that are not their own without being refused. A verifier that has never failed is indistinguishable from one that cannot fail; this one has now failed exactly once, on purpose. `claims/0023` is where those numbers live and where they can go red.
  **What this line owed, and what paid it.** This entry said it owed one design page and three RFCs, and named two sentences on `docs/design/deadline-all-the-way-down.html` that published what the tree had since reversed. **That debt is paid, and was paid before this line was re-read** — commit `54e9f28` rewrote section 04, which now carries the publish as four device operations with verify-before-accept (RFC 0060), the three collector invariants as predicates over named state (RFC 0059), and RFC 0058’s granularity. The sentence about an edit near the start of a large file still stands and is immediately followed by *“The bound is measured, and it is not universal”* — the 786 432-byte figure, and RFC 0062’s theorem naming the class it is false on. A published sentence with its own scope beneath it is not an unpaid debt; it is the shape this project asks a design page to have.
  Recorded because the entry was wrong in a way worth naming rather than quietly correcting: **a body that describes work as outstanding after it has landed is the same defect as a page that publishes a number it no longer holds**, and this file polices the second everywhere. Ten sentences in this entry described a tree that had moved, and the one that mattered most claimed `E2-B09` was not built while `blob/src/extent.rs` was 31 566 bytes of it.
  *exit:* met, and the second clause is met in the only way RFC 0062 leaves available. *A million blobs written, read back and verified*: `claims/0023` is `gating` — 1 000 000 records written and verified, and the falsification control that makes a verification mean anything, one flipped bit refused, reproduced by `cargo xtask claim blob-verification-refusals`. *An edit near the start of a large object re-chunks only a bounded region*: the bound is `CHUNK_MAX_BYTES` + `RESYNC_BOUND_BYTES` = 786 432 bytes, independent of the object’s size, on 32 of 32 drawn (seed, mixture) pairs. RFC 0062 proves no chunker reaches exactly-periodic content below `CHUNK_MIN_BYTES`, and **the answer to that class is the second object kind, which is built and measured**: `blob/src/extent.rs` replaces every piece an edit touches whole, so the cost is `2 × EXTENT_BYTES` however large the extent — `claims/0017` carries 2 097 152 at 8 MiB and the identical 2 097 152 at 128 MiB, sixteen times the object and the same number. The earlier refusal to tick this box rested on two reasons and both are now false: `E2-B09` is built, and the design-page debt is paid.
  What this line does **not** close, because it is a different sentence: `claims/0017` stays `pending`, and `E2-B09` stays open, because that claim’s denominator is an application byte as `intent/0006-state/spec.md` defines one — a byte a client submitted on the objects ring — and the ring answers `READ` and not yet `WRITE`. The bound above is a property of the chunker and the extent, measured at the store; the ratio is a property of a client, and is `E2-B09`’s. **Both of those closed on 2026-09-21 and this sentence is kept rather than deleted, because what it names is exactly what it took.** The ring answers `WRITE`, every edit on the extent kind crosses a real `f_objects::service::Service`, and `claims/0017` is `gating` at `application_bytes_written_at_the_ring` = 5 324 800 — a count taken by the component that was submitted to, printed beside the count this workload drew and required to equal it.
- [x] **E2-B02** `L` The zoned mapping: sequential fill, seal, copy-forward, reset — the collector and the device agreeing for once.
  The device is reached the only way anything is: over the blk driver's ring, from user space.

So the exit does not wait on an image; it waits on a host that can present a zoned block device. That is not hardware anybody has to buy: `modprobe null_blk zoned=1 zone_size=64 nr_zones=48 zone_nr_conv=4 blocksize=4096` matches `claims/baselines/linux-6.x-tuned-zoned/device.conf` row for row. It is not available *here*: the kernel under Docker Desktop is WSL2's, and it reports `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_ZONED=y` with `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_NULL_BLK` and `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_ZONED_LOOP` both unset, so there is no module to load and the container is unprivileged besides.
  **It stays work rather than debt, and `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md` says why at the one place that had asserted the opposite.** RFC 0093's four categories are what a *virtual machine* may not answer for, and any ordinary Linux host settles this one. From seed 1: **10 of 20 data zones reset, 11 `ZONE_FINISH`, 10 `ZONE_RESET`, 138 `FLUSH`, 1 293 824 bytes copied forward, and `device_bytes_per_app_byte` = 1.1336** — 1.0111 of fill, 0.1225 of copy-forward, the rest root records. RFC 0066 is the decision to land it above the frame with no caller rather than inside the frame with one, and it names the two things holding it there: **both of those sentences have since stopped being true and the conclusion has not.** The restart policy *has* left the frame — `decide` is `f_supervisor::policy::decide`, and the RFC 0008 row that said otherwise is paid and gone from `OWED_REVERSALS`, so citing `lint-owed` for it now points at a row the lint does not print. Nothing with a machine in it is rendered — no bus count, no scan order, no timing — because those are exactly what two runs of one generation may legitimately differ in.

The test shuffles the bus through eight seeded Fisher-Yates permutations — eight distinct orders — and gets one digest, and provokes the run-time half of the same refusal beside the compile-time one. Without that row the first demonstration would publish the same number on a machine whose scan never varied.

The manifest schema went to 3 on RFC 0030's stated terms, and the boot module's layout moved from `xtask/src/generation.rs` to `abi::boot::Module`: the condition its own comment named for moving it was a reader, and this crate is the reader.
  **The caller landed, and it is a component rather than the frame — which is RFC 0066's own prediction to the letter rather than a departure from it.** `user/supervisor/src/assemble.rs` links `f-assembler` behind an `image` feature and calls it from the supervisor's own boot path: the frame writes the boot module's address, its length and the root it selected onto the supervisor's board, and the supervisor refolds the tree with `f_generation::fold`, recomputes each member's content address over the bytes that arrived, resolves the routes, and walks the start order as `SPAWN`s on its control ring. And the guard that decides whether a place was already filled asked *the run* rather than *the row*: a run that skipped every member leaves every tally untouched and is indistinguishable from a run that did nothing, so a held-open place stayed empty while the report said it had not. `Assembled.filled[]` is per-member and `Assembled.submitted` counts what actually reached the ring, because `report.started` counts members `Start::start` answered `Ok` for — and a skipped member answers `Ok`.

- [x] **E2-B06** `L` Generation swap: instantiate alongside, transfer state, swap routing at a quiescent point, retire.
  **The protocol is `abi/src/swap.rs` and it holds no memory**, which is the decision inside this build rather than a detail of it: RFC 0063's two phases as a state machine over two `Declaration`s, the routing word between them, and the five ways phase A may be abandoned. The frame and the simulator are two implementors that must agree to the letter, and a rule written twice is a rule they can disagree about with a client's work held. `Routing` is the fifth cross-core word RFC 0016 says needs an argument — a place is not per-CPU and cannot become one — with one `Release` store, one `Acquire` load, `mutate-relaxed-routing` as the defect and `ring/tests/litmus.rs` as the harness.   **Registered as `claims/0029 operations-across-a-place-swap`, `gating`, fourteen rows and every zero paired with something that can move it.** `clients_observing_anything_but_latency` = 0 over 8 replacements of 4 components, 384 operations settled under load, never fewer than 4 in flight at any pause — and `operations_redone_in_place` = **0** beside `operations_redone_by_restart` = **24**, which is the only place in this tree where RFC 0063's central sentence is the same counter over the same client for both routes. Four of the row names are RFC 0012's own; its other three — `generation_swaps`, `reboots`, `frame_changed` — are deliberately absent rather than printed as zeros, because a zero under those names would read as *this ran and found none* when the truth is *this cannot run yet*. `claims/0021 generation-swap-pause` stays a reservation: the pause is a timing and this container may not record one.

Then a client submits against a `SetId` an instance that no longer exists answered, and gets its bytes. A restart answers `NO_SUCH_CAP`, and `cargo xtask blk killed` is that control in the same file with the same client, because RFC 0012 requires the two never be summed. **Three deficits, and all three are paid as of 2026-09-20.** *The window is bought*: it was `frames.alloc_zeroed` — a page from the global allocator, charged to nobody — at `SWAP_WINDOW_MAX` unconditionally, while `Swap::window_bytes()` had no caller under `kernel/`, so `lint-manifests` was checking `record_bytes * records_max <= memory_bytes` against a window nothing sized from it. It is `charge()` against the place's account now, sized by the declaration, and charged only for a manifest declaring `in_place`. **It is the *place's* and that is forced rather than chosen**: `Table::refund` is a watermark and can only give back the top, so a page outliving a generation has to sit under that generation's frames. The first draft charged it in `demonstrate` after the place had spawned; tearing generation 1 down rewound the watermark over the window and the successor was handed that page as its board — `stopped::NO_ROUTING`, nothing refused and nothing faulted. *The routing word is read*: `Routing::delivering` and `Routing::open` had no caller anywhere under `kernel/`, so RFC 0016's fifth cross-core word was a store with no load and delivery decided by whether `place.occupant` happened to be `Some`. `connect` consults it now and a connect arriving during phase A pends rather than being handed a channel to an instance mid-hand-over — which needed the word written where a place starts and stops delivering (`spawn` commits, `tear_down` pauses) and not only where a swap does.
  ***Alongside* is paid, and the design is smaller than the blocker made it look.** The frame tore the outgoing occupant down and *then* spawned the incoming one. It now takes the outgoing instance out of the place's slot and keeps it **alive** — table, address space, memory and the state it just wrote all standing — spawns the successor beside it, and retires it only after `Swap::commit`. **The outgoing instance never needed to be addressable, only alive**, so the slot being empty answers `spawn`'s refusal while the instance answers the abandonment's, and the 32-site split of `Place::occupant` that looked mandatory was not: one hoisted `Option<Instance>` and the place's previous identity beside it were the whole change. Phase A is reversible for the whole of itself — an abandonment at the acknowledgement puts the occupant back and ends the successor instead, restoring the place's module and manifest so its spawn refusal keeps comparing against what its occupant is actually running.
  **Two costs, measured rather than absorbed.** The outgoing instance's frames are charged *below* the successor's, so `Table::refund` cannot take them when it is asked to; **RFC 0095** defers the refund onto the place and applies it when the successor is itself retired, bounded at one because `f_abi::swap` admits one incoming instance and a second deferral is a refusal. `retire_instance` is extracted from `tear_down` for it, because `tear_down` takes the occupant *out of* the place and so by construction can only ever end the one. And **the boot processor's stack went 128K to 256K**: `demonstrate` carries a second `Instance` now, and at 128K it overflowed into the guard page — surfacing as `EXCEPTION 8` raised while the *second place* was being admitted, nowhere near the swap, because a stack overflow surfaces at whatever call happens to be deepest. Borrowing rather than moving the instance did not recover it: **there was under one instance of headroom left** on a stack whose own comment already said every place added since had been added to it and that the guard page had been catching it. It was, and this is what it caught.

What the first run of it showed was that the reversal was not worth what RFC 0063 promises, for two separable reasons, and the half asserted that rather than reporting a green tick.

So the boot has no `blk client` line at all, and the half now asserts that absence: nothing was submitted, nothing was lost. The earlier assertion said in its own failure text that it would be the stale one the day this landed, and it was. **Three things had to come with it and every one was found by a boot rather than by reading.** The wait must *serve* the ring it waits on — a replay re-does each deed and a registration asks the frame for a translation, so the first draft was a frame waiting on a component waiting on the frame, and the boot died as *a core was given a place's occupant to run and never reported back*. The client must be *wired* before it is driven — `Placed::serve` returns early on a zero `control`, so a client that learns its ring addresses from its first submission serves nothing. And the leak baseline must be *per generation* — a first fix guarded it on `free_before == 0`, generation 2 kept generation 1's reading, `retained` counted one generation's frames twice, and an equality check called the difference a leak.

The needle is `stopped::HANDED_OVER` in `user/virtio-blk/src/component.rs` now, because the residue is a component that ends where it should pause. **Why a pause costs a core, which is the part that had to be decided:** `f_virtio_blk::driver::Driver` — which owns the `f_ring::registry::Table` every client's `SetId` names — is a *local inside `serve()`*. Its lifetime is the call's. So an occupant that returned and was re-entered would rebuild that table empty, which is a restart wearing a resume's name, and a pause therefore means never leaving `serve()` — which means keeping the stack, which means keeping the core. RFC 0097 takes that and refuses re-entry for two reasons rather than one: moving the table into the heap makes a component adopt memory it did not initialise, which is RFC 0037's problem with no peer to blame, and moving it into the frame reverses what RFC 0008 moved out. **What it costs is one more application core for the length of phase A, on the boots that swap** — `-smp 2` stays pinned everywhere else — and a machine with one application core cannot abandon a swap without a restart, which is a fact about that machine and the reason `restart_only` exists. **The implementation is scoped and not built, and the scoping was re-read against the code on 2026-09-21 — one of the two things it called plumbing is not needed at all, and the part it called the work is larger than it said.** *Not needed:* `xtask`'s `emulator` does **not** have to take a core count. One thing a build may leave alone, stated so it is a decision rather than an omission: a phase-A failure *after* the pause — an unreadable record, an incompatible plan, a window that will not take the journal — may leave the core parked, because every one of those returns `Err` and the boot goes red without reaching `M0 ok`. And **an image modified to report the old digest defeats it entirely**, because a self-hash is a claim by the thing being measured: what boot 3 catches is a modified image booted *honestly*. The residual is E5's hardware root of trust, and release 0.3's attestation story is where it must be stated rather than implied.

  **The decision is taken and it is RFC 0098.** A `WRITE` edits the object its channel is about, at `offset`, and answers with that object's *new* content address — a name is its content, so an edit makes a different object and a client that could not learn the new name would have lost the old one. **The channel carries the subject because the ABI leaves no alternative**: a `Read` names its object by content address and fits, `32 + 4 = 36` against a `PAYLOAD_BYTES` of 40, and a `Write` cannot at `32 + 8 + 4 = 44`, with the payload one stride for every opcode. Where a channel has no object, a write at offset zero **establishes** one — which `Extent::create` does without a piece buffer, while `Extent::write` allocates `EXTENT_BYTES` whatever the object's size. So a boot establishes and can never edit, and `user/objects` **refuses** a non-zero offset rather than approximating it: a service that met an unaffordable edit by storing the submitted bytes would answer `Ok` to a client whose object it had silently replaced, which is what the first cut of this arm did. **What that leaves for this line is construction rather than discovery**: `bench` takes `f-objects`, `rechunk.rs` submits `op::WRITE` through `Service::answer` against a `Landing` — the shape `user/objects/tests/reads.rs` already builds — the service's write path holds an `Extent` where a mebibyte is free, and the denominator becomes `Served::written_bytes` instead of a figure the bench computed for itself. Both halves of the ratio then come off the ring, at this claim's own geometry.
  *needs:* E2-D02, E2-B01
- [x] **E2-B10** `M` The heap in `ring/`, which three files name as their blocker and no line owned.
  **Found as an orphan rather than planned as a task.** `intent/0006-state/plan.md` promised `ring/src/heap.rs` would land with `E2-B03`; `E2-B03` is `[x]`; `ring/src/` has no `heap.rs`; and `user/objects/src/lib.rs`, `user/assembler/src/lib.rs` and this file each name it as what they are waiting for. A promised layer with no owner is the decay `E0-P16` and `E0-D10` both exist against, and this line is the owner.
  **The design is decided and not re-opened here.** `intent/0006-state/spec.md`'s decision 4 says the heap is `ring/`'s — a bump-then-free-list over a granted region, built by a safe constructor that checks the region the way `ring/src/device.rs` checks a window, under RFC 0033's *a granted window is a safe accessor, and a heap is a window with a policy*. It is in `ring/` because RFC 0001 forbids `unsafe` above the frame and a `#[global_allocator]` is an `unsafe impl GlobalAlloc`.
  **What was open was where the allocator's mutable state lives**, because `xtask` refuses a component image with any writable symbol — the text page is mapped read-only, so a mutable global is a fault in a component that cannot report one — and `#[global_allocator]` needs a `static`. The answer is that the static is an *address* and the state is the region's own first bytes: free blocks carry their own links, so there is no side table and nothing wants `.bss`.
  **Two facts the whole design rests on were measured before any of it was written**, on 2026-09-11, with a throwaway crate built the way `xtask` builds a component: a `#[global_allocator]` **does** compile in a crate that declares `unsafe_code = "forbid"` when the `unsafe impl` is in a crate that permits it; and the resulting rlib has **no symbol in any of the eight classes `xtask` rejects** — only text. The allocator ABI shims are generated into the rlib as text rather than left undefined. Both were open questions and both are answered yes.
  **The blocker nobody had noticed, and it is closed.** No evidence for this could have been produced while `xtask` passed `-Zbuild-std=core,compiler_builtins` at every call site, because no image that links `alloc` compiles without it. The component profile passes `core,compiler_builtins,alloc` now — `xtask/src/main.rs`, one call site of four, and the three that do not are the kernel builds, which is the split the design asked for. Three components link a `#[global_allocator]` through it.
  *exit:* **met, both halves.** `ring/src/heap.rs` is the mechanism: `AT`, `GRANULARITY`, `describe`, `Heap::COMPONENT`, and the `live`/`peak`/`starved` triple. `ring/tests/heap.rs` is eight tests over a host arena — first fit takes the lowest address that fits, a split leaves a remainder only when the remainder is a whole block, a free coalesces with both neighbours, a request past the region is refused *and recorded*, and an alignment wider than the granularity is refused **without** being recorded as starvation, because a reader has to tell those two apart.
  **The control passes and it is the one that matters.** `a_free_list_is_a_function_of_the_live_set` builds one live set by two different histories and requires the next allocation to land at the same address. Address-ordered first fit with immediate coalescing is what makes that true; the test is what would go red if somebody made the list insertion-ordered for speed.
  **The caller was the missing half and it is no longer missing.** `f_ring::heap::describe` has two callers in the frame — the spawn-from-manifest path in `kernel/src/component.rs`, after mapping the granted region, and `kernel/src/process.rs` — and `kernel/src/process.rs` carries `const _: () = assert!(SPAWN_HEAP == f_ring::heap::AT);`, which is the assertion this entry said whoever closed the line would close it with. It is what stops the frame’s address and the component’s drifting the way `f_store`’s two did until `E1-B15`.
  And `user/objects` is a component image rather than a library: it is in `COMPONENTS`, its `manifest.toml` declares a `heap` need, and the topology names it. The boot is the evidence, and it is gated rather than printed — `run()` reads the heap triple back out of the serial log and fails if less than a page was described, if `starved` is set, or if `peak` is zero, which is the check that stops this passing on a boot where nothing allocated anything while a component declares a `heap` need.
  **What it cost, since A-05 asks which way the number moved and why.** `ring/` is 204 of 6 597 code lines unsafe, 3.1% — the heap adds a little and the share is unchanged, because a bump-then-free-list over a region the frame granted touches raw memory in one place and is ordinary code everywhere else. The tree is 6.1% and the frame 15.9%, both down about two points from when this entry was written, and the reason is the denominator rather than the numerator: E1 and E2 put code above the frame, which is exactly what RFC 0001 said would move this number and the direction it said it would move.
  *needs:* E2-B01

### Prove

- [x] **E2-P01** `L` Crash-consistency torture: a power-cut model in the simulator, cutting at every write boundary in a publish.
  **This is gate G2's headline property, and it is now registered as one: `claims/0025 cut-outcomes`.** The gate's third sentence is *cut power at every write boundary in a publish and never observe a state that was not one of the two intended ones*, and that claim is the sentence as a number — `cuts_leaving_a_third_state` = **0 over 108 280 cuts**, bounded at zero in both directions because there is no rate of third states that is not the property being false. A design producing one third state in a hundred thousand cuts would not be 99.999% atomic; it would be a design whose publish is not atomic, and the hundred thousand would be the measure of how long somebody's afternoon is before they find out.
  **What one cut is, because the strength of the number is in the model rather than in its size.** A publish is recorded once as a sequence of device operations. Every cut point then lands every operation a completed `FLUSH` covers plus a drawn subset of the rest, in a drawn order, with the cut falling inside the last operation — at block *and* byte granularity, in `honest` *and* `lying` mode — and each of the 108 280 resulting media is replayed onto a device **rebuilt from scratch**, mounted, and required to answer exactly one of the two intended root hashes at exactly the expected generation. Sixty-four seeds, every cut point of every one of 28 publishes, 483 841 device operations replayed. The re-run key is `(seed, target, cut, granularity, mode)` and every draw derives from it, so a finding is one line and not a hundred thousand.
  **The strict reading is what makes the zero worth reading.** A root record whose `check` field verifies over a generation tree that is not on the device is a third thing wearing the new root's name: it names a state the machine cannot produce, and the first read after it answers *not found*, which is indistinguishable from *collected*. So the answer is taken from `f_zone::mount::mount` — the highest generation whose check verifies **and** whose generation tree fully resolves. The 3 234 roots refused by resolution in lying mode are cuts that would have been third things under the weaker reading, and 847 torn records were refused by `check` at byte granularity.
  **The claim carries the control, and says why the headline property cannot see it.** `cargo xtask cut --mutate` arms `mutate-root-before-blobs` — the root record appended without waiting for the blobs it names, RFC 0060's first barrier removed — requires the sweep to find it, and then runs the reproduction line the red half printed both armed and disarmed. It was found at generation 1, cut 11 of 12, block granularity, honest mode. A root over missing blobs is refused by resolution and the mount rolls back, **which is the design working**; what catches the defect is the property the barrier is actually about — in honest mode no root is ever refused for non-resolution — so `claims/0025`'s `[baseline]` states the control as a second sentence rather than letting a reader take it for the claim's own.
  **The claim's route was proved able to go red before it was published, because on this branch that is no longer optional.** `cargo xtask claim cut-outcomes` runs the wide sweep and compares **every** row of the claim's own `[threshold]` table against what the run printed; a row the workload does not print is a finding rather than a silence. It was checked by breaking it: `cuts_swept` tightened to 108 281, `torn_root_records_refused` to 848, and a row named `roots_refused_unresolved` that nothing emits — three findings, the command red, all three reverted. `claims/0017` spent a whole task green over a bench its route could not reach and `claims/0018` was audited for the same shape one wave earlier, so the route is `xtask`'s shared `claim_compare` and `bytes-rechunked-per-byte` now runs through it too.
  *exit:* met. The exit is *"every cut leaves either the old root or the new one, never a third thing, across a full sweep of cut points and seeds"*. *Every cut leaves either the old root or the new one*: met — 108 280 of 108 280, 103 158 leaving the old root and 5 122 the new one, each mount required to answer one of exactly two hashes at the expected generation. *Never a third thing*: met, and met on the strict reading rather than on the reading that would have been easier to pass — a root that verifies but does not resolve is counted as a refusal and not as an outcome, which is why the number that could have hidden a third state is printed beside the one that could not. *Across a full sweep of cut points and seeds*: met — every cut point of every publish, both granularities, both modes, 64 seeds, with the four required observations checked rather than hoped for. Registered as `claims/0025 cut-outcomes`, `gating`, reproduced by `cargo xtask claim cut-outcomes`.
  **What this does not close, named rather than left to be inferred.** The device is `ZonedMemory` under a recorder in the host: it cannot lose a write the model did not draw, misdirect one, or return a block nobody wrote, so this is evidence about the *format's* response to a cut and not about any device's behaviour during one. `claims/0025`'s `[workload]` says so. And RFC 0013's *what would reverse this* names E2's crash-consistency work as the evidence that could reverse per-node atomicity; that section has now been read against this sweep and is **not** met — the sweep mounts a quiesced device, which is the case that RFC covers — and `docs/rfc/README.md` carries the reading, because entries there are append-only.
  *needs:* E1-P01, E2-B01
- [x] **E2-P02** `M` Property tests for chunking and deduplication.
  **Committed before the chunker and not compiling**, which is the discipline `docs/sdlc.md` gives a bug fix, applied to a bound: a property written after the chunker tests the chunker. Five properties over eight recorded seeds and four content mixtures — uniform, zero-filled, periodic with a period below the target size, and concatenations — with the mixture *iterated* rather than drawn, so a failure on one is not a matter of which mixture the draw happened to pick. Uniform bytes alone would have let a chunker that is bad at zero-filled content pass without anybody seeing it.
  **It falsified the spec's bound on its first run, which is the whole reason a property goes first.** Seven of thirty-two (seed, mixture) pairs never re-agreed at all; the first is seed 2, periodic, an object of 2 170 343 bytes and an insertion of 94 537 bytes at 828 916, where the sequences agreed again only at the object's end against an allowance of 1 447 741. The mechanism is phase and not density: `CHUNK_MIN_BYTES` made a cut *the first candidate at least 16 KiB after the previous one*, which is a rotation on a periodic candidate sequence and carries a phase offset forward forever — the model showed both orbits cycling the spacings [66 825, 67 734, 69 276] indefinitely. The spec's diagnosis was inverted for that family: it blamed the maximum, and the minimum was the cause. RFC 0061 is the reversal, argued from the measurement and from a removal that was measured rather than imagined.
  **RFC 0061 is implemented exactly as decided, and its own confirming run does not confirm it.** Acceptance is now a predicate over a window — one `MASK_BITS` = 16 mask drawn under the label `f-blob mask v2`, a candidate accepted when no candidate occurs in the preceding `CHUNK_MIN_BYTES`, the forced cut at `CHUNK_MAX_BYTES` the only surviving reference to the previous boundary. Properties 1, 2, 3 and 5 hold. Property 4 holds against the **published** bound on 32 of 32 pairs, zero violations — on an effective sample of 18, the count paid for on 2026-09-06 and recorded in the paragraph below — and the tighter flat clause is met *exactly* on 12 of the 13 pairs where it applies. What fails is the requirement the entry's *Consequences* state for its confirming run: that the flat clause and not the starved clause carry every periodic and concatenated pair. It carries 8 of those 16 — periodic seeds 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 and concatenated seeds 4, 5, 7 pass only on the starved allowance. That is RFC 0061's first reversal condition, fired.
  **The reason is the entry's arithmetic and not a coding error, and it inverts one of its sentences.** On content of period *p* the register after 64 bytes is a pure function of the last 64 bytes, so it takes at most *p* values and recurs every *p* bytes; the expected number of mask hits over a whole object is therefore *p*/2^16 ≤ 1 and the candidate set is all-or-nothing — density 1/*p* if some residue hits the mask, empty otherwise, with P(empty) = e^(−*p*/2^16), averaging 0.632 over the drawn periods and measuring 5 of 8. The failing periodic objects carry 0, 1, 1, 1 and 3 candidates across 2.2–3.8 MB and are genuinely starved. So RFC 0061's *periodic content whose period is below the target size leaves [the starved class], which is the measured repair* is **false**, and worse for the entry: `MASK_BITS` = 16 made periodic content *more* starved than the 14-bit loose mask it retired, where P(empty) was about 0.22 — which is why the entry's own earlier measurement saw periodic content with candidates at all. The next design it names is local-maximum chunking with the *w*-byte lookahead buffer it refused, paid for this time by a measurement.
  **What the run measured, for whoever writes that entry.** Mean interior chunk against a 65 536-byte target: uniform 78 396 over 300 chunks, zero-filled 262 144 over 87, periodic 90 230 over 256, concatenated 108 344 over 216, aggregate 108 063 over 859 — inside the factor-of-two band, so property 2 passes, against the 84 152 the entry predicted arithmetically for uniform content, i.e. the arithmetic was 7.3% high because the maximum truncates the tail. Interior chunks forced at the maximum, per ten thousand: uniform 166, periodic 2148, concatenated 2222, zero-filled 10 000. The uniform 1.66% is well inside the entry's one-in-ten reversal condition, and it is reported with no threshold because the entry says a threshold invented before the first measurement is a description. Zero-filled is exactly `CHUNK_MAX_BYTES` on 87 of 87 interior chunks, which pins the gear table and the new mask together: if that assertion goes red, the register's fixed point on a zero run has started hitting a mask and every object hash ever written has changed.
  **One reading of `starved` had to be fixed, and it is flagged rather than buried.** A maximal candidate-free run is a property of the object's candidate sequence and not of where the edit landed in it, so the walk is seeded at the last candidate at or before the edit rather than at the edit. The forward-only reading misclassified seed 7 uniform — an edit at 846 791 inside a 359 549-byte candidate gap, both streams forcing a cut inside it at different offsets and reconverging at the gap's end — as unstarved, purely because the next candidate happened to be 168 866 bytes further on. Both readings leave the 8 failures untouched.
  **Three debts an adversarial review raised against pull request #29, paid 2026-09-06, all of the same shape: the branch reported more than it measured.** None of them moves a constant, a seed, a mixture or a rule, and none touches a decision — RFC 0062's starved clause and RFC 0061's acceptance rule are exactly as decided. (1) Property 4's headline `agreed <= allowed` could not fail on 14 of the 32 pairs — 8 zero-filled, 5 periodic, 1 concatenated — because their allowance reaches past the edited object's own end, and on every one of those 14 `agreed` *is* that end; the count now has the row `resync_pairs_where_the_allowance_exceeds_the_object = { min = 8, max = 28 }` in `claims/0017`, its floor being the exact count of zero-filled pairs and its ceiling what the unstarved positive control leaves, and the effective sample of **18** is stated beside the 32 in the claim, in `blob/src/chunk.rs`, in the test and here. (2) Property 5 asserted nothing on 13 of 32 pairs, 5 of which deduplicated 0 bytes of 2 MiB of shared content, because `owed` is the shared run less an allowance that covers all of it; `dedup_pairs_with_a_non_zero_requirement = { min = 4 }` is the row, derived from the eight uniform pairs the way `resync_pairs_unstarved` is, and the choice against asserting a floor on the starved pairs is argued in the property's own doc comment: RFC 0062's theorem says two placements of a period-below-16-KiB run share no chunk, so a floor above zero there would be a number the design cannot meet. (3) `claims/0018` is `status = "gating"` and said its thresholds were asserted in property 2; counted exactly, one of its eleven rows was — the aggregate band — and a second was asserted as a ratio rather than as its own `min = 87`, which is one gap more than the audit counted. All eleven are now, including `forced_cuts_per_ten_thousand_uniform` — RFC 0061's third reversal condition, one interior chunk in ten on uniform content, which until this diff could have fired inside a green `cargo xtask verify` with nothing to observe it. Each new gate was checked by mutation rather than by argument: at 100 per ten thousand property 2 goes red at 166, at 13 the vacuity ceiling goes red at 14, at 20 the deduplication control goes red at 19, at 5000 the geometry row goes red at 859 interior chunks, and the mutations were reverted. RFCs 0061 and 0062 are not edited — `docs/rfc/README.md` says entries are append-only — so the effective sample sits in the claim, the code and this file, and their *32 of 32* stands as what those entries reported on the day they were written. The five properties have all passed since `18da1e2`; the sentence in the exit paragraph below about `cargo xtask verify` being red on property 4 predated that commit, and re-verdicting an exit was not that diff's to do. The paragraph below is that re-verdict, taken on 2026-09-07 against RFC 0062's *Consequences* and against a green `cargo xtask verify`.
  *exit:* partly met, and what changed to get here is the verdict rather than the tree. The exit is *"an edit at offset X re-chunks a bounded region around X and nothing after it, for randomly generated edits and object sizes"*, and RFC 0061 did not edit that sentence — it added a section saying what the sentence means under the new bound, and split it in three. *Re-chunks a bounded region around X*: met for content that produces candidates, at `CHUNK_MAX_BYTES` + `RESYNC_BOUND_BYTES` = 786 432 bytes, independent of the object's size, on 32 of 32 pairs. *And nothing after it*: met outside a starved run and false inside one, where every boundary is forced, a forced cut is by definition relative to the previous boundary, and two streams offset by *L* do not resynchronise until the run ends — on a wholly zero-filled object everything after the edit is re-chunked and this test asserts exactly that. *For randomly generated edits and object sizes*: met, over eight seeds and four mixtures. **What made the previous verdict *not met* was RFC 0061's requirement that the flat clause and not the starved clause carry every periodic and concatenated pair, and RFC 0062 withdrew that requirement** — on arithmetic showing no rule this design permits can meet it — replacing it with four assertions keyed to the object's own candidate sequence instead of to a mixture label: the published bound on 32 of 32, the tight clause on the 13 pairs it applies to, carriage restated over starvation rather than over labels, and the all-or-nothing structure of the candidate set on periodic content. All four hold, all five properties pass, and `cargo xtask verify` is green — the sentence that used to stand here saying it was red on `the_boundary_sequences_resynchronise_within_the_bound` has been false since `18da1e2` and is withdrawn rather than left standing. Nothing was weakened to reach that: no constant moved, no mixture dropped, no seed removed, no `#[ignore]`; `claims/0017`'s replaced row went to `max = 0` and three exact counts rather than to a wider number; and property 4 collects all 32 pairs and fails on the collected list rather than panicking on the first, which reports more and not less. **The middle leg kept this `[>]`, and both things it was waiting for now exist.** *And nothing after it* is false inside a starved run, and RFC 0062's theorem says why it will stay false: every exactly-periodic object with a period below `CHUNK_MIN_BYTES` = 16 KiB is starved under every rule this design is willing to consider. That is not a defect a test can drive out, and no widening of the window, mask or lookahead repairs it.
  This entry said the design's answer to that class was RFC 0058's second object kind, *“which `E2-B09` has not built”*. **It is built.** `blob/src/extent.rs` replaces every piece an edit touches whole, so the cost is `2 × EXTENT_BYTES` however large the extent, and `claims/0017` carries the measurement the closing condition asks for: 2 097 152 at 8 MiB and the identical 2 097 152 at 128 MiB — sixteen times the object, the same number, which is the clause *bounded by the copy-on-write granularity and not by the object's size* as a pair of figures rather than an assertion.
  So this closes against the theorem and against that measurement, which is what its own sentence said closing it would take — and not against a property that could still go red here. The same false sentence stood in `E2-B01`, for the same reason and about the same file; a body that describes work as outstanding after it has landed spreads, because the next entry cites the first.
