- [x] **E2-P03** `M` Collector invariants as properties: nothing reachable is swept, a reset zone holds no live blob, collection never starves a deadline-class read.
  **All three are asserted as predicates over named state, and I1 is asserted twice over.** RFC 0059 wrote them as predicates precisely so that this task could assert one rather than watch for a symptom, and `zone/tests/invariants.rs` asserts them at the instants that RFC states: I1 at the instant `ZONE_RESET(z)` is submitted, I2 and I3 at every step boundary. I1 is checked once as the mechanism the RFC says implies it — every root in `R` marked, every bit of `B` in the condemned zone clear — and once as the formula `blobs(z) ∩ reach(R) = ∅` over a walk taken from the store's own hash-to-block map rather than from the collector's bitmap. **A wrong mark satisfies the mechanism and fails the formula**, which is the difference between a collector checking its own homework and a collector being checked.
  **Two qualifications the builder stated, and this line keeps both rather than rounding them up.** *First*: **"concurrently" is a deterministic interleaving and not threads.** RFC 0004 puts ordering behind `f_env::Env`, so *concurrent* here means *interleaved at a stated granularity* — one collector step, which is at most one device operation plus bounded computation and is the unit RFC 0059 says a cycle is a sequence of, with one drawn client action between each pair, drawn through `f_env`'s `Scheduler`. Threads would not be a stronger test here; they would be the policy violation, and they would publish a number nobody could re-derive. Two runs of one seed interleave identically, which is what makes a failure a reproduction rather than an anecdote. *Second*: **the artefact is `zone/tests/invariants.rs`, which is where `intent/0006-state/plan.md` names it, and not `sim/`, which is where the spec's sentence puts it.** The build followed the plan; the difference is recorded here rather than resolved, because a property asserted in the crate whose state it is about is the cheaper artefact and moving it to `sim/` would be a decision with an owner rather than a tidy-up.
  **The workload is adversarial in the four ways the exit's own words ask for**, and the fourth had to be found rather than written: allocation racing collection, a multi-zone publish held open across two `ZONE_RESET`s — the case the first invariant's open-publish clause exists for and the one the plan names — an unpin mid-cycle, and a hard-class reader that is a real read as well as a queue entry. **I3 is unfalsifiable without a backlog**: `Queue::collector_operation` drives one operation at a time, so with a backlog of one the witness is bounded by the device's chain length whatever the queue's ordering does, and the run stayed green on a queue whose ordering had been replaced by arrival order until the backlog existed. That was measured rather than argued, and it is why `collector_backlog_entries` is a thresholded row.
  **Registered as `claims/0026 collector-invariants`, `gating`, and every row in it is one of the run's own vacuity refusals written as a number.** `collector_operations_ahead_of_hard_read` = **1**, pinned in both directions — below it the bound was never approached and above it the invariant is false — and RFC 0059 derives that 1 from `f_virtio_blk::pending::IN_FLIGHT`, which `zone/tests/cycle.rs` checks against the driver's own source so the row moves when `E1-B09` raises it and in the same diff. Beside it: I1 asserted at 38 reset instants, I2 and I3 at 4 926 step boundaries each, 38 resets of which 8 were taken with the multi-zone publish open, 10 resets held by the guard's third conjunct, 414 hard-class entries of which 185 read bytes back and verified them and 232 were submitted behind queued work, 687 collector operations queued as backlog, 34 zones spanned by the held publish. `cargo xtask claim collector-invariants` compares every one of those against the claim's table and treats a row the run did not print as a finding.
  *exit:* met, with the two qualifications above kept in the record rather than dropped. The exit is *"all three hold while collection runs concurrently with adversarial allocation and a hard-class reader"*. *All three hold*: met — I1 at every reset instant as mechanism and as formula, I2 at every step boundary over every zone, I3 with its witness at the bound and not below it. *While collection runs concurrently with adversarial allocation*: met, on the reading RFC 0004 forces — a seeded interleaving at one collector step, with a publish that opens mid-cycle, a multi-zone publish held open across sweeps, and an unpin that schedules the full re-mark. *And a hard-class reader*: met — 185 of the 414 hard-class entries read bytes back through the map and verified them against their own name, and 232 of them were submitted with work already queued, which is what gives I3 anything to be about. The artefact is the crate's own test target, which is `intent/0006-state/plan.md`'s reading; the spec's `sim/` is the difference this line records.
  *needs:* E2-B02, E2-D03
- [x] **E2-P04** `L` Verus on the frame's invariants, now that the frame has stopped moving.
  **This entry named five blockers on 2026-09-06 and every one of them has gone or was miscounted — re-read 2026-09-20.** It said `cargo xtask lint-owed` reports *five reversals fallen due and unpaid, four of them in the frame*: `OWED_REVERSALS` has **three** rows and all three are in the frame (`abi/src/door.rs` twice for RFC 0014 and RFC 0015, `kernel/src/blk.rs` for RFC 0051). It led with *the restart policy `E1-B05` left there against RFC 0008*: that row is **paid** and the table says so in place of it. It named the ring, the doorbell and registered buffers as `[>]` in E0: `E0-B12`, `E0-B15` and `E1-B10` are all `[x]`, as is `E1-B14`. So *proof against a moving target* no longer describes this task, and what is left is the work itself plus one decision.
  **The decision is RFC 0096 and it was the task's real content.** RFC 0053 fixed what a proof here may be — *a bounded proof over the file the kernel ships, not over a copy of it and not over a model of it* — and mechanised it with `#[path]`, one file compiled twice. **That mechanism does not transfer.** Kani verifies ordinary Rust, so an unannotated second compile is a complete input; Verus verifies only what is written inside `verus!{ … }` with `requires`/`ensures`/`invariant`, so the same compile gives it nothing to read. Either the shipped file carries the annotations, or there is a model beside it — which is what 0053 refused by name. 0096 takes the first and states the price: `f-kernel` gains `verus_builtin` and `verus_builtin_macros`, in the one tree RFC 0001 concentrates `unsafe` in.
  **What was established by probe rather than assumed**, because the old entry's blocker list is what happens when it is not: the development container reaches the network (`api.github.com` answers 200 from inside it), Verus publishes an x86-linux release so the checker is obtainable exactly as Kani is under RFC 0022, and `vstd` is `no_std`-capable — `#![cfg_attr(not(feature = "std"), no_std)]` — with its long list of unstable features gated on `verus_keep_ghost`, which is set when *Verus* compiles it and not when rustc does. So the kernel's ordinary build asks far less of it than the verification build.
  **The sharpest risk was tested rather than written down as a risk, and it passed.** 0096 named it as *that `verus_builtin` compiles under `nightly-2026-08-01` for `x86_64-unknown-none`* — Kani never asked a runtime crate of its own to build under this tree's pin, and `CLAUDE.md` forbids bumping the toolchain as a side effect. Both `verus_builtin` and `verus_builtin_macros` build clean for that target under that pin as ordinary dependencies of `f-kernel`, pulling `verus_syn`, `verus_prettyplease`, `syn`, `quote`, `proc-macro2`, `convert_case`, `synstructure` and `unicode-ident` behind them. The probe was reverted, because a dependency with no caller is not one this tree keeps. What survives is narrower: cargo resolved against *latest Rust 1.99.0-nightly compatible version* and both crates are published by date rather than semantically, so an exact `=` pin is part of the work and the failure would otherwise arrive as a toolchain bump nobody asked for. **The part no reasoning settles was settled by running it, on 2026-09-20.** Verus is in the `full` image — a pinned release, `verus --version` asserted so a broken bundle is a red layer rather than a checking job that silently verifies nothing — and it was pointed at `kernel/src/cap.rs`'s `refund`, reduced to its arithmetic and given two postconditions, one that should hold and one that should not. It answered `1 verified, 1 errors`. **The floor property verified; the conservation property was refuted**, because `refund` does `slot.extent = slot.extent.saturating_add(bytes)` and so `object + extent` is not preserved — a refund whose `extent + bytes` would pass `u64::MAX` loses the difference silently. So `verus!{ … }` works under `#![no_std]`, it **verifies rather than parses**, and it found something structurally unreachable to the checker this tree already has: `kernel/proofs/src/mem.rs` sets `FRAME_SIZE` to 256 and the harnesses run an eight-slot table, so `u64` overflow is not in Kani's space and never will be. RFC 0053's own reversal says a bounded check is *a weaker statement kept for its speed rather than for its content*, and this is the first result in the tree that shows that difference rather than arguing it. **Whether the overflow is reachable in a running frame is a different question and is not claimed**: `extent` is bytes left in an untyped region and no machine here has one that large. What the refutation establishes is that the precondition was never written down. **Two costs were paid rather than estimated.** Verus pins its own rustc and demands it through the rustup shim — the first build failed at `verus --version` with *toolchain 1.98.1 is not installed*, which is this task's `two rustc versions must coexist` arriving as a number — and `unzip` belongs in the `full` stage, because putting it in the base invalidated every layer under it including Kani's 483 MB bundle. **What is left is the exit itself**: the proofs must be over the shipped file rather than a reduction of it, there must be an `xtask` route that runs them clean and again with a mutation armed, and the nightly must run it — which `PROVE_RUN_GAP` already says this machine cannot establish.
  **Built on 2026-09-21, and the file is smaller than the entry above expected.** RFC 0096's decision is *the shipped file carries the annotations*, and the cost it priced was the whole of `cap.rs` becoming a file two compilers read. It is not. Verus runs a compiler front end over its input, so a file saying `use crate::` needs the crate around it — the stand-in machinery `kernel/proofs` carries for Kani — and a file that names nothing above itself does not. `kernel/src/watermark.rs` is the three functions an untyped account's watermark is moved by, arithmetic over four `u64`s and nothing else; `verus` reads it as a crate root exactly as it sits on disk, with no second crate, no `#[path]` and no stand-ins, and `cap.rs` calls into it.
  **Three properties, and two of them corrected the code they were written for.** `carved`: carving a frame moves the base up and the remainder down by the same number, so `object + extent` does not move — `Table::retype` did that with two `wrapping_` calls under the comment *“Checked immediately above, so neither of these can wrap”*, and what was checked immediately above is `extent >= FRAME_SIZE`, which bounds the subtraction and says nothing about the addition. `refunded`: the conservation RFC 0096's own probe refuted on 2026-09-20, now stated and held. `carvable`: the predicate `Table::grow` selects an account by, so that a selection saying yes cannot be followed by a charge saying no — it used to be one of its two clauses. **Neither correction is claimed to be reachable**: `extent` is bytes left in an untyped region and `object` is a physical address, and no machine here has either within a frame of `u64::MAX`. What the refutations establish is that the preconditions were never written down, which is a different and checkable claim.
  **The defect is in `DEFECTS` and deliberately not in `MUTATIONS`, and that is the whole argument for a second checker.** `mutate-saturating-refund` takes the overflow guard out of `refunded`; no boot provocation finds it, because no machine here has an untyped region within a refund of `u64::MAX`. `cargo xtask verus` arms it and requires the conservation postcondition to be refuted, in `watermark.rs`, at `refunded` and at no other property — three phases, the third being a `cargo check` of the armed build, because nothing else in this tree ever compiles that feature and a defect that had stopped compiling would keep passing phase two for ever. **`kernel/proofs` reaches the same file through `#[path]`**, so both checkers read one source: Kani at `mem::FRAME_SIZE` = 256, Verus over every `u64`. A fourth stand-in was refused for RFC 0053's reason — a copy of the only file two checkers read would drift, and the first thing it would stop containing is the specification.
  **RFC 0053's and RFC 0057's last reversal conditions were owed here, were read, and are *fired and not met*.** Both say a deductive checker arriving would make the bounded ones a weaker statement kept for speed. Verus arrived and discharges none of the invariants either entry is about: the five capability properties are about a table reached through a raw pointer, `adopt`'s are about 640 bytes a peer wrote, and a deductive proof of either needs a specification for memory. What *is* now a run rather than an argument is 0053's own sentence — both preconditions this work found are `u64` overflows that `kernel/proofs/src/mem.rs` puts out of reach on purpose. `docs/test-taxonomy`'s `safety-comment-hollow` row had the same reversal and is narrowed the same way: the checker is here and it reads no `SAFETY` comment, because `watermark.rs` contains no `unsafe` at all.
  **What it costs, paid rather than estimated.** `f-kernel` and `kernel/proofs` each take `verus_builtin` and `verus_builtin_macros`, pinned with `=` because both are published by date rather than semantically; they pull seven proc-macro crates behind them and none is in the image, because the frame leaf `cargo xtask generation` measures is the linked artefact. The frame compiles for `x86_64-unknown-none` under the pinned nightly with `-D warnings`, which was this task's sharpest risk and was tested rather than assumed. One line was not forecast: `use verus_builtin::*` is load-bearing under Verus and has no user under rustc, so it carries an `allow(unused_imports)` with the reason beside it.
  *exit:* met. *The chosen invariants are proved*: met — three, over every `u64`, in the file the kernel ships rather than in a model beside it, and the choosing is written down where it can go stale loudly: `VERUS_PROPERTIES` names each one and the run refuses a file that has stopped stating any of them, so a deleted property is a red run rather than a smaller green count. *The proofs run on a schedule*: met — a second step of the nightly `prove` job, in the same job and not a new one because `image_full` is the only image either checker exists in and `proof_schedule` refuses a second dependent; two checkers behind one image is one blast radius. *A mutation to the frame fails them*: met — and it is the clause with the strongest evidence, because the mutation is one `cargo xtask mutate` cannot reach and the run requires the refutation to land at the named property in the named file rather than merely requiring a red exit. **What is not met and is not claimed**: that GitHub runs the job, and that the deductive half runs on anything but x86-64 — Verus publishes no aarch64-linux bundle, so an arm64 `full` image has Kani and not Verus. Both are `PROVE_RUN_GAP` rows now, printed on every green `lint-proofs`.
  *needs:* E0-B12, E0-B15, E1-B05, E1-B10, E1-B14 (the last so the batching landed with a number stays landed, and none of the four still `[>]` re-shapes what the proof states)
- [x] **E2-P05** `M` Whole-system state comparison: two runs, two hashes, and a diff that descends to the divergent subtree.
  **The fold is a second hash and the module says so.** `sim/src/whole.rs` folds every component's published region to one SHA-256 root — the component's key, its node count, and each node's id, kind and unit beside its word. `f_abi::state::snapshot` is untouched and still answers over bytes, because that one is about *one region read by two readers of different ages* and this one is about *are these two runs of one commit in the same state*. The descent is `Divergence` and deliberately not `Option<Node>`: that signature cannot express two roots differing with every node standing still, so a `None` there would report agreement about a pair that demonstrably does not agree — `generation/src/diff.rs` made the same argument one layer up.
  **Registered as `claims/0031 whole-system-divergences-localised`, `gating`.** `injections_localised_to_the_exact_node` = **64** of `injections_attempted` = 64, through `f_abi::state::write_word` so the format keeps one writer and the harness knows the answer before it asks; `whole_system_roots_at_one_seed` = 1 taken across two *processes* rather than two calls, `whole_system_roots_across_two_seeds` = 2 as the control, and `component_trees_diverged_when_the_class_was_disarmed` = 1 of 2, which is the scenario's own `clients: 2` argument being taken — an allocation failure is one component's. The route was checked by breaking it twice: a fold that stops hashing the words makes both seeds give one root, and a descent that names a neighbour passes every earlier phase and arrives at 10 of 64.
  **RFC 0013's reversal condition was owed here and is answered here: not met.** That entry's *what would reverse this* names E2's state comparison as the evidence that could reverse per-node atomicity, and `docs/rfc/README.md` recorded the question as owed to this task. The comparison reads a component's tree **quiesced**, one component at a time, so nothing in these 64 injections or in the disarmed run turned on two nodes read at one instant. Per-node atomicity stands, and what could still fire it is a comparison taken of a machine under load — which is the frame's half below.
  **The boot half is paid, and it was one board slot and four `set` calls rather than a mechanism.** `f_abi::state::Writer` has shipped since `E1-B15` and `user/store` has used it from above the frame with no `unsafe` all along; what was missing was that a *place's* occupant had no way to know where its tree was. `component::spawn` maps `process::SPAWN_TREE` writable and argues for the writability in its own comment; `process::prepare_driver` and `prepare_server` map the control ring, the board, the registers and the queues and not that page. So the address had to come off the board — `routing::at::TREE_AT`, written by the place path and written as an explicit zero by the account-less one — and a component publishes only when it is told. **Taking the constant instead would have faulted at ring 3 on six boots with nothing in the fault naming the cause**, which is the failure `user/store/src/report.rs` already records this split causing once.
  **The frame takes the second reading, and that is what makes it evidence.** `mounted_line` prints the snapshot of a tree whose component has not run — a constant per manifest, and deliberately a fixture so the boot log stays reproducible. Its own doc forecast this day in as many words: *the day a component writes into its own tree before this line, it stops being one*. It still is one, because it is printed at the mount; the new `state after` line is printed after the occupant's core comes back and carries both readings, and `state::node::COMPONENTS_MOVED` counts the trees where they differ. `cargo xtask blk served` prints `snapshot 0xe542117a927c963b, moved since the mount read 0x40d69e0cf0f65c45` and the frame's own tree carries `moved = 1`. **RFC 0065's closing measurement — at least one mounted tree's snapshot differs from the snapshot of the same tree at the moment it was mounted — is met**, and it is read through the frame's root rather than asked of the component, which is RFC 0013's arrangement.
  **`WHOLE_SYSTEM_GAP` narrowed rather than went, and its own condition is what fired.** It said *whoever teaches a component to write into its own region deletes this constant in the same diff*. A component does, and the constant is still there with different text, because deleting it would have replaced one overreach with its mirror image: `supplied_place` gives a core to exactly one place's occupant, so one mounted subtree of five is live and four are the constants the old text was entirely about. Two boots compared would diverge on one and be blind on four. `claims/0031` gains **no boot row** for the same reason — one live subtree is a different measurement from 64 injections localised 64 times, and `E2-P08`'s line records what summing two harnesses costs.
  *exit:* met, and the split that used to be stated is now on the other side of the line. *An injected divergence is localised to a named subtree automatically, with no human reading a log*: **met** for a run's whole-system state — one command, an exit status, and a node named by a machine that computed it, 64 of 64 by exact node and never by the subtree containing it. **Met for a boot's too, one subtree wide, and the width is stated rather than rounded up.** The sentence that stood here said nothing writes a word into the mounted regions, so a boot's subtrees are constants and a comparison over constants cannot diverge. `user/virtio-blk` writes four counts it already keeps into the region the frame mounted for it; the frame reads that region back through its own root after the occupant's core returns; `state after … moved` and `COMPONENTS_MOVED = 1` are the reading. What is **not** met is breadth, and it is a different clause from the one that held this line open: one occupant is scheduled per boot, so four of five mounted subtrees are still constant, and a boot-to-boot comparison would diverge on one and be blind on four. That is `WHOLE_SYSTEM_GAP`'s narrowed text, printed on every green `cargo xtask compare`, and it closes when a second place's occupant gets a core — which is work in this tree and not a blocker outside it.
  *needs:* E0-B14, E1-P01, E1-B15 (a whole-system state is one only when every component publishes into it)
- [x] **E2-P06** `M` Reproducible builds verified across two machines and two dates.
  Not from nothing. `E0-R01` already measured that a from-scratch kernel build at one path reproduces its content address, and its `package` and `address` jobs compare two runners at one commit. What this adds is the second date, the generation root as the thing compared, and a job that names the non-reproducible input rather than reporting a mismatch. `docker/README.md` records the gap that stays open: the image is repeatable but not reproducible in this task's sense, and the root hash is produced by the build rather than the image, which is why the gap is smaller than it looks and still a gap.
  **The first deliverable was the remap, and it was a measurement rather than a precaution.** `cargo xtask generation` at `/work` and at a second checkout path — same commit, same image, same volumes — gave two frame leaves and two roots: `c76453507cb0126b…` against `d03b72f8ca82a289…`, and `b142e37c0d250736…` against `65494a4f5b8ec18b…`. All four component leaves and the topology hash were identical in both, so the divergence was one leaf and it had already named itself. That is exactly the path-dependence class `E2-B04`'s rehearsal left untested and whose own line said so. `.cargo/config.toml` now carries `-Zremap-cwd-prefix=.` and the two paths give one root. **The flag is written three times and the repetition is the design:** cargo *replaces* `build.rustflags` with `target.<triple>.rustflags` rather than merging them, so a remap in one list only would cover every crate in this tree except the one image the frame leaf is taken over, and the component build sets `RUSTFLAGS` in the environment, which replaces both. `-Zremap-cwd-prefix` and not `--remap-path-prefix`, because the stable flag takes a literal prefix and the prefix wanted here is wherever the tree happens to be, which a committed file cannot spell; it costs the nightly channel, which `rust-toolchain.toml` already pins, and the reversal is a move to stable at which point the prefix is computed by whoever spawns cargo.
  **The naming half is met and demonstrated rather than argued.** `generation/src/diff.rs` descends two checked trees leaves-before-roots, so a red run names the input that moved instead of reporting that two roots differed; the plan wrote it `Option<Leaf>`, and that signature cannot express every leaf agreeing while the roots do not — the case that names the compiler instead of an input — so `Divergence` is that signature widened by exactly the cases a leaf cannot carry. Nine tests, each asserting what is *named* rather than that something was found. `cargo xtask generation --mutate` is the half that says a green comparison means anything: it arms `mutate-path-in-image`, which compiles `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` into the frame — `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and not `file!()`, because the remap already covers `file!()` and a defect the fix covers demonstrates nothing — and then **requires the two-path comparison to go red *and* to name `kernel` by name**. Red alone would satisfy an exit code and prove nothing, which is the argument `MUTATIONS` makes for every boot.
  **Registered as `claims/0028 generation-roots-across-paths`, `gating`, and both halves are one invocation because the armed run overwrites the artefacts the honest one wrote.** `generation_roots_across_two_checkout_paths` = **1**, and with the defect armed **2**; `armed_comparisons_naming_the_frame` = 1; `checkout_path_length_difference_bytes` = 51, which is `ELSEWHERE`'s own argument made checkable — two paths of equal width would let a remap that swapped one prefix for another of the same size leave two images a byte comparison cannot tell apart, and the check would pass for the wrong reason; `leaves_folded_into_the_root` = 5, read back out of the emitted tree rather than counted from the source, so a generation that lost its members cannot agree about nothing and be reported as agreeing. Each root is read out of its artefact by `distinct_roots` rather than inferred from a function not returning an error, because a row that reads 1 for that reason says only that the function did not return an error. `cargo xtask claim generation-roots-across-paths` compares every row; the route was checked by breaking it — the headline inverted to `{ min = 2, max = 2 }` and `leaves_folded_into_the_root` tightened to 6 — two findings, the command red, both reverted.
  *exit:* **met on the half a command can answer, narrowed under RFC 0093.** *A non-reproducible input fails the job and names itself*: met, and demonstrated rather than asserted — `cargo xtask generation --mutate` arms a defect that compiles the checkout path into the frame, the comparison goes red, the report names `kernel`, and the harness requires that leaf **by name** rather than merely requiring a red exit code. A check that accepted any red would pass on a build that failed for an unrelated reason.
  *Identical generation root hash, checked weekly, across two machines and two dates*: **struck and kept in the register.** `--elsewhere` separates the checkout path out of the bundle a second machine differs by and says nothing about the rest — one host, one kernel, one filesystem, one uid, one clock. `lint-remap` prints that limitation on every green run rather than leaving it to be discovered, and the weekly job’s `root` matrix is where the two-runner half would land if there were two runners.
  **The path half was not a formality, and that is the argument for closing on it.** Before the remap landed, the same commit at `/work` and at a second checkout path gave two frame leaves and two roots, with every component leaf and the topology hash identical — a real divergence, in exactly the leaf where it would have mattered most, found by the only half of this task one machine can run.
  *needs:* E2-B04 (there is no generation root to compare before the evaluator produces one)
- [x] **E2-P07** `M` Rollback test. **Half of gate G2.**
  **There is no boot menu, and the spec settled that rather than inventing one.** The menu is the loader's: `f.root=<64 hex>` beside `f.frame=<64 hex>` on a multiboot command line, a list of modules under QEMU, and the `menuentry` block `cargo xtask generation --install` writes for a loader this repository does not contain — no GRUB is imported, because GRUB is GPLv3 and a demonstration does not cross the licence boundary. The grammar is `abi/src/boot.rs`, whose one sentence named the day the frame would parse it; the *rule* is `generation/src/select.rs` and not the frame, because `kernel/` is `test = false` and a refusal only a boot can reach is a refusal nothing checks.
  **The clause to be strict about is *bit-identical*, and a matching root is not it.** The fold covers the record tree and none of the component files travelling in the same module, so a correct tree beside a file that is not the one its leaf names folds to the same root. Three comparisons are made and each is named where it is made: the root (necessary), the module digest taken **on the machine** over the bytes that arrived, and the generation rebuilt from source after the break. The third is what makes this a rollback rather than the re-selection of a file nobody disturbed.
  **Registered as `claims/0030 rollback-comparisons`, `gating`, and the tampered module is why the digest earns its place rather than being asserted to.** `comparisons_matched` = 3 over `boots` = 6 and `module_bytes_delivered` = 65 348 across 4 component files; `leaves_moved_by_the_break` = 1, named by `f_generation::divergence` rather than spelled `"kernel"` here; `unknown_roots_refused` = 1, because a machine that fell back to whatever module it had would pass every other row while booting something nobody asked for; and the three `tampered_*` rows = 1 each — a module that folds to the honest root, is selected by the frame under it, and is caught by the digest and named at member 0 by `f-assembler`. The break is `mutate-unchecked-index`, a real defect reused from `cargo xtask mutate` rather than a byte this command flipped to have something to roll back from. The tamper is one byte of a component's *image* and not of its manifest record, which was found rather than designed: filling the file with a run of one byte made it stop being a manifest and the assembler answered `NotAManifest`, a shape refusal that was never in doubt, before the content check could run.
  **The claim is `gating` and something now runs it, which was not true when this line was first marked done.** `cargo xtask rollback` was in no workflow and not in `verify` — three generation builds and six boots is why neither took it — so half of gate G2 rested on a command nobody ran, which is the state `xtask/src/main.rs` names in as many words: *a gating claim that nothing in the local loop runs is a claim that gates nothing.* `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`'s `rollback` job runs `cargo xtask claim rollback-comparisons` nightly, which compares every row of the `[threshold]` table rather than only running the workload. The residual is a scheduled job's own and is written in the claim: nothing opens an issue, and a red cross on a schedule is easy to stop seeing.
  *exit:* met. *Break the system deliberately*: met, with a defect that is argued for where it lives and lands in exactly one leaf. *Roll back from the boot menu*: met on the reading the spec settled — the token selects out of a menu of two, and a root nothing on the menu carries is refused by name rather than defaulted to. *Verify the restored generation is bit-identical to what it was*: met on all three comparisons, and the third is the one no re-selection could pass. What is **not** demonstrated and is not claimed: nothing has been booted from a disk through a loader's own menu — `docs/booting-on-hardware.md` and `docs/second-boot-outside-qemu.md` carry that gap — and this is a reboot rather than a generation swap, which is `E2-B06`'s half and `SWAP_GAP`'s.
  *needs:* E2-B05
- [x] **E2-P08** `M` Live-swap test. **The demonstration half of gate G2's second sentence, in the simulator.**
  **One client, and unchanged.** `chaos::Load` is the client, because *dropped*, *doubled* and *answered wrongly* already have definitions in that crate and a second client would be a second opinion about what a client observes. Four components, two replacements each, 96 phases each under sustained load, every replacement begun with operations in flight. The transfer is verified at three levels and not one — a check word over the record's other twenty-eight bytes, refused by the *reader*; two live-registration counts taken through two real registration tables on opposite sides of the window and required to agree; and the client's own ledger — which is what the exit's *rather than assumed* asks for.
  **The three negative controls are in shipped source rather than in a patch**, because a harness whose zeros have never moved is reporting on its own wiring: `hasty` lies to the protocol about the drain and loses 73 of 96 phases; `garble` flips a byte of the window, the record's reader refuses it and the swap abandons naming `refused`; `amnesiac` hands over nothing while declaring `in_place`, every step of the protocol succeeds, and the *client* is refused for a set the incoming table never received. The numbers are `claims/0029`, registered on `E2-B06`'s line.
  *exit:* met, in the simulator, and the boot is not available to be measured. *Replace a running component under sustained load*: met for every component this tree's manifests declare, checked against the manifests rather than against the build output. *No client observes a dropped operation*: met — nothing dropped, doubled, answered from a retired occupant, answered wrongly, or refused unretryably, over 384 settled operations. *The state transfer is verified rather than assumed*: met, three ways. What this cannot reach is a swap **on a machine**: `SWAP_GAP` is the checked line, and gate G2's *replace a running component without dropping work* is paid here by a simulation of the frame's protocol and not yet by the frame. **Re-read on 2026-09-19, when a boot could swap.** That last sentence is withdrawn in the half it was about and kept in the half it was right about. `cargo xtask blk swapped` takes one place of one booted machine from generation 1 of `user/virtio-blk` to generation 2 and a client submits afterwards against a registration the retired instance answered, so the frame is no longer only simulated — but **this line's numbers stay this line's numbers and are not summed with that boot's**. 384 settled operations under sustained load across 8 replacements of 4 components is not comparable to one client and two reads, the two harnesses count different things, and `claims/0029`'s rows are registered against this one. What the boot adds is not a bigger number but a different subject: the thing swapped is a place rather than a model of one. What it does *not* add is `alongside` — the frame tears down before it spawns — so `SWAP_GAP` is narrowed rather than gone and still sits on `E2-B06`'s line, and the five ways `f_abi::swap::Abandoned` says phase A may be reversed are exercised here and only partly available there.
  *needs:* E2-B06
- [x] **E2-P09** `M` Change-point detection over the stored measurement history, replacing thresholds.
  The one E2 task startable before gate G1, and worth knowing what it would read. `E0-P11` stores a record per run with a schema version, and on every machine this project can reach the harness refuses to record timings — `F_ENVIRONMENT` is `container` or unset — so the in-tree history holds coverage counts and stated gaps, not distributions. The first half of the exit is satisfiable on synthetic history. The second half is not: "ordinary run-to-run noise over the same period" needs a gating claim that has run repeatedly on the named machine, and that begins at `E0-P06`.
  **The detector is built.** `cargo xtask history --changes` reads `claims/history.jsonl` and reports where the series stepped rather than what crossed a bound, which is `claims/README.md` rule 5 with something behind it at last. Medians and a median absolute deviation, both integer — not only because RFC 0004 rules out the arithmetic the usual measures reach for, but because a mean is dragged by one outlier and a measurement history’s outliers are exactly the runs where something unrelated went wrong on the machine. The median is what makes *one bad afternoon* not a change point.
  **The spread term is the part that is not a threshold.** A candidate step must clear the size asked about *and* exceed three times the larger window’s own scatter. Without the second it is a threshold wearing a different number, and `a_step_inside_the_noise_is_not_reported` is its own test because it is the property a later change is most likely to remove by accident — removing it makes every other test here pass more easily.
  Seven tests, and one of them was wrong first. With noise whose period did not divide the window, a nominal thirty-unit injection came out of the medians as **twenty-nine** and a test asserting *a 3% injection is found at a 3% bar* failed against a detector doing exactly what it should. **Injecting a 3% step and the medians differing by 3% are two claims**, and only the second is one a detector can act on. The noise in the tests is now period-aligned to the window so the medians are exact, which isolates the property under test — and the fact that real noise is *not* period-aligned is one more reason the second half of this exit needs a machine.
  *exit:* **the first half is met, the second is narrowed under RFC 0093.** *A 3% regression injected into the history is detected*: met, by `a_three_percent_regression_is_found` and by the verb against a synthetic history — 80.0 stepping to 77.0 is reported as `-37 per thousand` at the record it happened. *Ordinary run-to-run noise over the same period is not*: met **against the noise model the tests state**, and struck as a claim about **this system’s** noise, which is kept in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`. This entry said so before the work started — that half *“needs a gating claim that has run repeatedly on the named machine, and that begins at `E0-P06`”* — and nothing found since has changed it.
  It reports and does not gate, which is a decision rather than an omission: two coverage readings are not a series, and a check wired into `lint` today could not fail. The reversal is written on the function — when a claim’s own series reaches ten records on a recording machine, this stops being a verb somebody runs and becomes a line in the gate.
  *needs:* E0-P11, E0-P06
- [x] **E2-P10** `M` The write-amplification claim, on zoned hardware if available and on an emulated device clearly marked if not.
  "Against a tuned Linux filesystem" is a baseline, and a baseline is configuration in the tree or it is prose that decays — `E1-D06` is where that rule became a task, and this claim is the second one it has to configure a baseline for. The emulated device, if that is what runs, is QEMU's zoned virtio-blk from version 8; see `E2-B02` for the image base change.
  **The claim file exists as of `E2-B02` and this task is what puts a number in it.** `claims/0016` is registered `pending` with `emulated = true`, its thresholds stated — `device_bytes_per_app_byte` ≤ 1.5 from `SWEEP_LIVE_FRACTION`, `ratio_vs_baseline` ≤ 1.0 — and both of them measured by nothing: the modelled cycle's 1.1336 is a `modelled_*` row taken at the `ZonedDevice` boundary in the host, and the claim's own rows are counted by `query-blockstats` in a guest. Registering the thresholds before the measurement is deliberate; a claim registered without them is a claim whose thresholds get written to fit its first run. What this task still owes is the boot and the device. **Two of the three things this sentence used to name are gone.** It read *the boot, the QEMU 8 image and `claims/baselines/linux-6.x-tuned-zoned/`, which does not exist* — the directory exists and has for some time, with nine files and its own `verify.sh` cross-checking four workload dials against `zone/tests/cycle.rs`; and the image is trixie with QEMU 10.0.13, which turns out not to have been the obstacle at all. `E2-B02` above records what is: QEMU emulates no zoned virtio-blk in any version, only passthrough of a host zoned device, and this host has no module to make one with.
  *exit:* **narrowed out under RFC 0093 in its entirety**, and kept in `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md` rather than deleted. The exit is *bytes written to the device per byte written by the application, against a tuned Linux filesystem on the same device*, and every word of it needs a device that is really zoned. This machine cannot present one: QEMU emulates no zoned virtio-blk at any version and this kernel builds neither `null_blk` nor `zloop`, which is `E2-B02`'s blocker and is a machine rather than a purchase. **What was built in its place is the half that survives the wait**: `claims/0016` registered with both thresholds stated *before* any measurement existed, so they cannot later be written to fit a first run; and `claims/baselines/linux-6.x-tuned-zoned/` as nine files with a `verify.sh` cross-checking four workload dials against `zone/tests/cycle.rs` — a baseline that is configuration in the tree, which is `E1-D06`'s rule, rather than prose that decays.
  *needs:* E2-B02, E1-D06

### Release

- [x] **E2-R01** `S` **Release 0.3.** Storage and generation claims; the rollback and live-swap demonstrations; the attestation story.
  Carried no `needs:` and so ranked as ready to start before the epoch had a single task done. The blockers are its own contents and gate G2: the two storage claims, the two demonstrations, the attestation, and the power-cut sweep the gate names.
  **Three of the six contents exist as of wave 5, and the release should describe them as narrowly as they were built.** The rollback demonstration is `cargo xtask rollback` and `claims/0030` — a real defect, a token that selects out of a menu of two, and three comparisons where a root alone would have accepted a tampered module. The live-swap demonstration is `cargo xtask swap` and `claims/0029` — in the simulator, which is where the load, the seeds and the abandonments are — **with a boot beside it as of 2026-09-19**: `cargo xtask blk swapped` takes one place from generation 1 of `user/virtio-blk` to generation 2 while a client keeps the registration the retired instance answered. The release notes must carry both and must not sum them: 384 operations across 8 replacements is the simulator's number and one client over two reads is the boot's, and the boot's contribution is not a bigger count but a different subject. The sentence a release still may not write without qualification is *replaces a running component alongside its successor* — and **the reason given here was stale and is corrected on 2026-09-21**. It said *because the frame tears down before it spawns*, and the frame does not: `component::demonstrate` takes the outgoing instance out of the place's slot and keeps it alive while the successor is spawned beside it, which is that clause of `E2-B06`'s four paid. The qualification a release still owes is the **pause**: the occupant an abandonment puts back has exited its serve loop, so it cannot be made to serve again, and an abandoned swap costs that client a restart. `SWAP_GAP`'s needle is `stopped::HANDED_OVER` and RFC 0097 is the decision that is taken and unbuilt. The attestation story is `cargo xtask attest` and `claims/0032`, and its five residuals are RFC 0012's own: no signature, no verifier, no freshness, nothing about mutable data, and no defence against an image modified to lie about itself. RFC 0056 already requires a release to name what it does not contain; those five are the list for 0.3, and *attestation* must not appear in the release notes without them. **And one thing this release inherits rather than decides, because the decision was taken:** `claims/0030` is `gating`, and it now runs nightly — `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`'s `rollback` job, `cargo xtask claim rollback-comparisons`, thresholds compared and not only the workload run. The alternative was to hang it on this task's own release gate, and that was refused: a claim checked once per release is a claim that goes red at the worst moment. What is left for this task is the half a schedule cannot reach, which is its exit — *the rollback demonstration runs from the release image on a stranger's machine* is a stranger typing that command on a machine this project has never seen.
  *exit:* met but for the stranger, and **that clause is narrowed out under RFC 0093** into `docs/TECHNICAL-DEBT.md`. *The package reproduces*: met — `cargo xtask generation` folds to one root, `claims/0028` gates the comparison across checkout paths, and `lint-remap` prints on every green run what it cannot decide. *The rollback demonstration runs from the release image*: met as a command — `cargo xtask rollback`, `claims/0030`, gating and nightly. *On a stranger's machine*: **no commit can be that person.** It is the same condition `E1-R01` and `E0-R02` carry and the long plan's `G4`, deliberately outside the team's control. The three contents this release describes are each written as narrowly as they were built, including what a *failed* swap still costs a client, because a release that said *replaces a running component* without that sentence would be claiming more than `cargo xtask blk abandoned` shows.
  *needs:* E2-P10, E2-B09, E2-P07, E2-P08, E2-B07, E2-P01

> ### Gate G2
> Break the system deliberately and roll it back. Replace a running component
> without dropping work. Cut power at every write boundary in a publish and
> never observe a state that was not one of the two intended ones.

## E3 — The interface

*Phase 03. The longest and hardest epoch, and where most projects die. Budget
accordingly and narrow early rather than late.*

**Effort:** 4–10 person-years · **Risk:** high · **Ends at:** gate G3, release 0.4

*Where the tree stands against this epoch, checked 2026-09-17.* The
decomposition landed. `intent/0012-the-interface/` is `E3-00`'s deliverable and
the seventy-two lines below are its output, pasted under the eight parents they
decompose; five tasks in this epoch are done and six are ready to start.

***Re-read against the code on 2026-09-21, and the count in the sentence above
was three commits stale.*** The photograph was taken before the epoch's first
build round and never retaken. **Nineteen subtasks were built on 2026-09-16 and
2026-09-17** — `0fda44f`, `71df569`, `8d2423a`, three new crates, five `abi`
entry formats, one `interface` module, five RFCs and an `xtask` lint — and
every one of them was read afterwards by a reviewer whose job was to defeat its
exit sentence. Twelve exits hold. Seven do not. **None of that reached this
file**: seventeen lines read `[ ]` that a reader of the code would have called
done, and the seven refusals existed only in commit messages, which is the one
place a reader of `TODO.md` does not look. Both are corrected on the lines
themselves, and the seven now carry their findings rather than the fact that
findings exist — re-derived on 2026-09-21 by a fourth round, because round
three's twenty-one were never written down and a fifth reviewer would have paid
for them a third time.

**What that leaves the epoch reading, and it is worth reading as a shape rather
than as a score.** Seventeen `[x]`, seven `[>]`, sixty-nine `[ ]`. Of the
sixty-nine, **thirty-six need nothing this project does not have** — the
blockers that kept them off the available list are `E1-B05`, `E2-B01`, `E1-B04`
and `E1-B15`, and all four closed; and **thirty-two are a measurement no machine
here can take**, which is the register's business and not this file's. The
twelve that hold are also not a twelfth of the epoch's risk: eleven of them are
a wire format, a table or a corpus, and the twelfth is an arena. Nothing below
rasterises, nothing shapes a glyph, and nothing has yet been a compositor —
`E3-B01f` is the line where this epoch stops being libraries nothing links.

**And one line was missing rather than open.** `E3-B08` is specified in full in
`intent/0012-the-interface/plan.md`, argued in four places in `spec.md`, and was
never pasted here. It is added below. A decomposition whose own output is
incomplete is exactly what that line exists to catch, one level up. Until
that paste every line below carried no `needs:` outside the epoch, so
`cargo xtask todo E3` ranked thirteen of twenty tasks as ready — the same reading
E2 corrected for itself on 2026-09-06 — and the tool's answer is only ever the
graph's, which is why every parent below now names its children. What the graph
now says. Gates G0, G1 and G2 are all unpassed, with eight, nine and ten tasks
`[>]` behind them and releases 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 unshipped; a release that
precedes this one is on `E3-R01`'s line for the reason `E2-R01` gave. The claims
registry holds thirty-three rows — fifteen `pending`, seventeen `gating`, one
`tracked`, recounted on 2026-09-17 with
`grep -h '^status' claims/*.toml | sort | uniq -c` rather than carried over from
the last draft, and the denominator moved because this epoch's own three claims
landed in between. The fifteen are `pending` because `runner-class-A` (`E0-D10`)
is a name and not a machine and F has booted twice in a VM and never on metal
(`E0-P18`), and two of the fifteen are not times at all: `claims/0034` is a ratio
of integers and `claims/0035` a share, and both say in their own first paragraphs
that this is why they could gate where a timing claim could not. Gate G3 is a
time: input to photon at p99, taken by a photodiode, under load. So every prove
task in this epoch but one is a measurement on hardware nobody owns yet, and the
one that is not (`E3-P05`) replaces a UI suite that does not exist. The frame is
still moving, but less than this paragraph used to say: `cargo xtask lint-owed`
reports three reversals fallen due and unpaid — RFC 0014's and RFC 0015's door
retirements and RFC 0051's `Reported` — down from five, and the one that was paid
is the frame's, `policy::decide` having become `f_supervisor::policy::decide` on
2026-09-13. `E1-B15` is `[x]`, which is what moves `E3-B07`'s second clause out
of the hardware bucket and onto `E3-B07d`. `E0-B12` and `E0-B15` are still `[>]`
— for a number `E0-P05` owes and for a user-interrupt path QEMU's TCG backend
implements no part of — and a scene graph waits for neither: the ring's layout,
cursor protocol and suppression and the kernel's doorbell path are in the tree
and green, so the subtasks below carry what they actually need and `E3-B01` no

them, and `0008`'s finding is that E2's storage story is argued rather than
measured; opening a fourth epoch on top of that repeats what the audit named.
What this epoch has to stand on: virtio-gpu in user space (`E1-B04`), which

### Decide

- [x] **E3-D01** `L` The semantic node vocabulary, version 1 — small enough to be unambiguous, large enough that applications do not escape into opaque canvases.
  **Closedness is structural rather than tested, and that is the part worth checking first.** A `vocabulary!` macro in `interface/src/node.rs` emits the enum, `ALL`, `COUNT` and every per-role table from one list, so there is no second place a variant can be written. The two tests that tried to guard a hand-written array were both defeated — the second by `Role::Other => {}`, which is the loophole the whole decision exists to close — and deleting them was the repair rather than strengthening them. Twenty-two roles, no `Other`, no string role and no `#[non_exhaustive]`, because the web's annotation vocabulary failed on ambiguity rather than on size.
  **What the tree cannot observe about this decision is registered rather than argued.** `claims/0034-canvas-escape-rate.toml` is the metric that would say twenty-two is too small, and it states in its own first paragraph that it can only go red in one direction: a rate below the target is equally consistent with a corpus of applications too simple to push on anything. A vocabulary size is the kind of number that invites a defence, and the claim refuses to be one.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0077-the-semantic-vocabulary-is-closed.md` is accepted and expresses the settings panel, the file browser and the timeline with no node marked "other"; the canvas loophole is closed by `Defect::EmptyCanvas` rather than by convention. It departs from `ring-scene-boot` section 11 by one field, `parent`, and says what would make that wrong.
- [x] **E3-D02** `M` Canvas participation: what a self-rendering surface must still declare about its content.
  **The timeline was built to section 13's own falsification rather than to a case that would pass.** An agent selects the clip between 4.2 s and 6.8 s on track 3 and a screen reader describes it, neither of them seeing a pixel — the test the design page proposed against itself. Times are integers with the scale in the name, which makes a timeline's time base a decision this tree takes once rather than a formatting detail every surface invents; RFC 0004 leaves no other option.
  **What it does not settle is written into the RFC rather than left to be discovered.** Whether developers will declare structure they do not declare today is a usability finding, and no test in this tree can produce one. What can be enforced is the floor, and the floor is enforced: a canvas that declares nothing is a defect and is not admitted.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0078-a-canvas-declares-its-content-or-it-is-an-escape.md` is accepted and `interface/src/canvas.rs` makes the timeline case expressible — clips, tracks, times and a selection, addressable and scriptable while the pixels stay custom.
- [x] **E3-D03** `M` The typed design-token layer: what is themeable, what is range-checked, what a theme cannot break.
  **The sweep is what makes the guarantee safe to state.** `every_ground_admits_a_readable_ink` sweeps every integer luminance rather than a stride over the colour cube, which is how the true minimum of 4 582 at `#008909` was found lying between two swept greys — a stride would have reported a floor that does not exist, and reported it green. The contrast arithmetic is fixed point with the scale in the name, because the two architectures do not agree on floats.
  **The stroke's floor is derived rather than chosen, and no longer shared with spacing.** A theme with no space is dense; a theme with no stroke has deleted a rule the compositor was told to draw. One constant serving both would have made those two outcomes indistinguishable, which is the kind of economy that costs a category.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0079-a-theme-may-not-make-an-interface-unreadable.md` is accepted, `interface/src/token.rs` carries the three categories — themeable, range-checked, and out of a theme's reach — and a deliberately hostile theme is clamped rather than refused, demonstrated by test. `claims/0035-theme-refusals.toml` prices the policy the RFC argues.
- [x] **E3-D04** `S` The renderer fallback ladder, decided before it is needed.
  **Each rung's cost is registered rather than asserted, and the ladder reads the registry rather than a copy of it.** `claims/0033-raster-cost-per-rung.toml` holds the four thresholds and `interface/src/ladder.rs` binds to them with `include_str!`, so a number that moves in one place cannot stay still in the other. The CPU rung's threshold is derived from `ring-scene-boot` section 09's latency chain rather than from the rung above it — the correction RFC 0080 made out of its own first draft, which had quoted a ratio between two rungs and thereby made one rung's number an argument about another's.
  **Both reversal conditions compare a rung to a rung on a machine that started at one of them.** The earlier threshold-to-threshold form deleted the hybrid while it was winning and refuted the floor on speed it was never offering: a condition that can be met by arithmetic over two different machines is not a condition about the ladder.
  *exit:* met. `docs/rfc/0080-the-fallback-ladder-is-decided-before-it-is-needed.md` is accepted and names the four rungs in order — compute path renderer, hybrid, CPU, tessellating floor — with `claims/0033` carrying what each costs. A ladder decided after it is needed is decided under the pressure of a machine that cannot run the top rung.

A fourth round on 2026-09-21 did re-derive them, and this is what it found. The narrowed exit asks the lint to refuse every route into `third_party/` *that reaches code the lint compiles*, to prohibit three surfaces outright rather than inspect them, and to record a fixture for each of the three routes it admits it cannot see. **(1)** Three of the four routes round three named — a raw-string `#[path]`, a two-line `#[path]`, and `include!` — are still open, and they are open in `kernel/proofs`, `ring/proofs` and `abi/proofs`, the three directories the workspace `exclude`s. **(2)** No test calls `lint_boundary`: deleting any one of its three nets leaves the suite and CI green. **(3)** The *fixture recording each of the three routes it cannot see* clause is unmet — the three residues are prose in RFC 0092 and `LICENSING.md` and nothing more. **(4)** The build-script surface is prohibited at two spellings rather than at the mechanism, and both fall to one character. **(5)** *Prohibits … outright rather than inspecting them* is false of the configuration surface, which is inspected by substring. **(6)** RFC 0092 claims a repair that is not in the tree. **(7)** *Naming the two files that make the ring observable* is met for one file and not the second.
  *exit:* an RFC, accepted, naming which and what would reverse it, and — because it answers *imported* — naming the task that lands the entry and the two files that make the ring observable; and `cargo xtask lint` refuses every route from the permissive tree into `third_party/` **that reaches code the lint compiles**, and prohibits the build-script, symlink and configuration surfaces outright rather than inspecting them, with a fixture driving each route red and a fixture recording each of the three routes it cannot see. Narrowed from *every route … other than a ring* by RFC 0092, before the mechanism was built rather than after it: a line-oriented text matcher cannot make a universal claim over a grammar it does not parse, three review rounds defeated the old check by about one character each, and the three routes that survive the new one — a configuration `lint` never compiles, a proc macro reading an imported file at expansion, and a vendored copy taken by registry name outside `third_party/` — are named there rather than left as a remainder. Narrowed earlier from *if imported, `third_party/` carries the entry and `cargo xtask lint` shows the permissive tree reaching it over a ring and by no other route* by RFC 0088, because this is an `S`-sized decide task whose own decision fired a conditional asking for an import, a manifest and a `shape` protocol that no amount of deciding produces; the entry, the ring it is reached over and `"third_party"` in the root `exclude` move to the import task RFC 0082 writes out.

The lint is guarding a route before there is traffic on it, which is the right order to build in and the wrong thing to call met.
  *exit:* one time source in the whole path; a lint finds any second reading of a clock in it, with a fixture that makes the lint fail; under the simulator the stamp is `Env`'s virtual time and one seed gives one sequence of stamps on both architectures.

The unread-field half is closed twice over: `Reader::finish` requires the tail to be zero, and `Event::decode` compares all sixty-four envelope bytes against the ones `Event::envelope` writes field by field. The scale is in the field name, `stamp_nanos`, and an event that was never stamped is refused rather than defaulted.
  *exit:* **met.** fixed-width on both architectures, unread fields refused when non-zero, and the timestamp's scale in its name rather than in a comment.
  *needs:* E3-B04a
- [>] **E3-B04c** `M` Prediction forward to the next scanout.
  **Built, and its exit refused by adversarial review. The findings are written here because until 2026-09-21 they were written nowhere.** Three rounds ran on 2026-09-16 and 2026-09-17 (`0fda44f`, `71df569`, `8d2423a`); the fact of the refusal survived in a commit message and the reasons did not, so a fourth reviewer would have re-derived them. A fourth round on 2026-09-21 did re-derive them, and this is what it found. **(1)** *Over-prediction is bounded separately from under-prediction* is false of the instrument rather than of the predictor: `axis()` records a cursor drawn forward while the pointer went backward as the same sign as the opposite case, so the two bounds are not measuring the two things the exit separates them for. **(2)** The universal theorem carried beside the measured pair — *eighty times looser* — is decoration in two ways. **(3)** The exit cites RFC 0084 for a sentence RFC 0084 does not contain: the RFC records a one-clause narrowing and the line adds the ten-times draw. That is the hazard RFC 0084 created and was amended in round three to name — *a citation is cheaper to write than a decision* — occurring a second time, in a line that cites the amendment. **(4)** `Held::NoBaseline` is a guard that cannot fire, and its doc claims to close a trap that is closed two hundred lines earlier.
  *exit:* deterministic from a seed; over the named corpus the test sweeps — 4096 seeded recordings at two report rates — the error is bounded by an integer stated in the test, over-prediction is bounded separately from under-prediction because a user notices them differently, and the test draws ten times that corpus and is required to observe the over-prediction bound failing there. Narrowed from *over a recorded motion corpus the error is bounded by an integer* by RFC 0084, on a measurement: the integer is that corpus's own maximum rounded up to a pixel and not a property of the generator. Ten times the corpus runs the worst over-prediction from 7.20 px to 10.19 px at the recorded rate and from 7.90 px to 9.86 px at half of it — both past the stated 8, on fourteen of 81 920 measurements — while the two under-prediction maxima move by 4.6 and 5.1 per cent and are exceeded five times. So the tighter half of the pair, the half the module argues hardest for, is the less stable half. `input/src/predict.rs` states the pair over the named set, pins each number to the set's own maximum in a `const` so that widening it is a build error, and carries the one universal over-prediction bound beside it as a theorem over the speed and lead ceilings, eighty times looser. The unqualified sentence comes back only with a predictor whose over-prediction has a closed form over the input domain, which a first-order extrapolator of a measured velocity does not.

Findings 2 and 3 are each other's only support, which is why neither can be repaired alone. **(4)** The exit as it stands is not the sentence RFC 0084 authorises: the RFC's block quote ends earlier, and both added clauses are recorded nowhere — RFC 0084's own hazard, a third time. **(5)** The reversal the exit names, a seventh opcode `SET_EFFECT`, is charged to no line in this file, so the exit states a condition nobody owns.

And nothing requires a `Kind::Effect` node to declare anything at all: a node created with that kind and no declaration is accepted by `crate::arena` and `crate::commit`, which is a census neither of them keeps rather than a refusal this function could make. Both halves come back together with a seventh opcode `SET_EFFECT` carrying a node, an estimate and a saving, which is the reversal and is an ABI change.

## E4 — The platform

*Phase 04. The point at which somebody outside the team can build something.*

**Effort:** 2–4 person-years · **Risk:** medium · **Ends at:** gate G4, release 0.5

- [ ] **E4-00** `S` Decompose this epoch before starting it.
  *exit:* no `XL` task without a decomposition.

### Decide

- [ ] **E4-D01** `L` Interface definitions for every system service, with availability annotations from the first version rather than the third.
  *exit:* every service has one, and E4-P01 generates its compatibility matrix from them mechanically.
  *needs:* E0-D03
- [ ] **E4-D02** `M` The powerbox interaction: what a grant looks like to a person, given that the bet's stated failure mode is usability.
  *exit:* a person unfamiliar with capabilities grants access to one file and not to its directory, without being taught anything, with at least five participants.
- [ ] **E4-D03** `M` The package and generation model for third-party software.
  *exit:* a third-party generation is publishable and rollback-able by the same mechanism as a system one.

### Build

- [ ] **E4-B01** `XL` Component runtime and bindings for the chosen component model.
  *exit:* a component written in a second language runs, holds only routed authority, and is swappable without recompiling the system.
  *needs:* E4-D01
- [ ] **E4-B02** `L` The powerbox: pickers that are grants.
  *exit:* no interface exists by which a component asks for authority it was not routed; E4-P03 proves it.
  *needs:* E4-D02, E1-D01
- [ ] **E4-B03** `L` Developer tooling: build, run, debug, and read the state tree of your own component.
  *exit:* a newcomer goes from empty directory to running component in under an hour, timed with a real newcomer.
- [ ] **E4-B04** `M` Third-party generation publishing, content-addressed like everything else.
  *exit:* an outside build produces the same hash as ours, verified by E4-P04.

### Prove

- [ ] **E4-P01** `L` The compatibility matrix: current against N-1 peers in both directions, plus refusal tests below the floor.
  *exit:* green in CI; a deliberate incompatible change fails it with a `PEER` error naming the missing feature.
  *needs:* E0-D03
- [ ] **E4-P02** `M` A conformance suite generated from the interface definitions.
  *exit:* a component claiming an interface must pass it; a deliberately non-conforming component fails on the specific clause.
- [ ] **E4-P03** `M` The capability negative suite at component scale.
  *exit:* the five M4 properties hold across the full component topology, not just against the kernel.
- [ ] **E4-P04** `M` An outside party reproduces a build and gets the same hash.
  *exit:* done by someone with no access beyond the public tree, and their transcript is published.

### Release

- [ ] **E4-R01** `S` **Release 0.5.** The SDK, the conformance suite, the compatibility matrix.
  *exit:* published with a getting-started path that E4-B03 timed.

> ### Gate G4
> Someone outside the team ships something, against a negotiated ABI, without
> reading the kernel source.
